1.2. Chapter 19: Season 2 Ep 3 (英语原文)

"Okay..." Kenichi was the first to speak as the music died out with Subaru's exit towards the light and the last appearance of Echidna played out. "Can someone explain to me... Everything that just happened? Please?"

He was pleading to the wrong people as everyone in the room dealt with what they'd seen.

"Apologies, we don't mean to be insensitive to you, Natsuki-san." Otto, who was hanging his head low after what they'd watched, was the one trying to show a little diplomacy.

"I didn't blame any of you for being insensitive, man!" Kenichi tried to stop his frustration from showing in his tone but failed. "Why was there music blasting over... what we've just seen... like some... some freaking anime?!"

Many gave confused looks at this question, including nearly everyone in the Emilia and Crusch camp, while Anastasia looked more interested than ever by the man's wording.

"... Us mercenaries and civilians dunno what such a thing is..." Ricardo endeavored to say from the back with a wince, seeing that the parents weren't really in the mood. "Sorry..."

"How come you ignored it?" Noako asked, her eyes on Emilia and Rem with a clear need for answers, yet no hint of accusation. "Did this... Has it happened before? I heard someone say that this was 'another'..."

"Um..." Emilia swallowed, trying to shuffle her hand slowly to Subaru's sleeping sleeve as she worked up the courage to speak with his mother. "Y-Yes. Every time one of these 'viewings' begins or ends... We see these visions with music playing in the back... It's... Unsavory to say the least."

"Not that the melodies themselves are bad, though..." Felt muttered from the front, which caused Heinkel to snort from the back.

"Melodies, my fuckin' ass..." Heinkel was bitter as he said this. "Ain't none of what we've watched so far is deserving of praise. Much less some fucking weird-ass... musical outlet while the kid's suffering. How many times has this happened before I was summoned in 'ere with the kid?" He pointed to Schult while glaring down at the others.

"It's happened since the very beginning, I suppose." Beatrice narrowed her eyes at the floor, remembering the very first viewing.

The parents exchanged what was the first of many glances at each other before they moved their eyes to Subaru's sleeping face.

"You don't think..." Kenichi's voice broke slightly as his wife held on to him tighter. "No... There's no way... He was dying in that opening..."

"Opening?" Crusch aims a questioning gaze at the two adults. "Is that what it's called? How'd you come by such information?"

Though she didn't mean to sound too demanding, the duchess wasn't the only one interested, as Anastasia piped from the other side of the same row to speak with the parents on her own.

"Couldn't hurt t' help us get knowledge about these things, Natsuki-kun's folks." She said with a bit of excitement in her eyes. "You two are from a different world than us Lugunican bunch... And we want what's best for yer son... So?"

"What's best for my son?" Kenichi looked at the merchant queen with eyes full of hesitation, which made Anastasia lean back with an eyebrow raised.

The parents both seemed to be contemplating what they'd watched, which led them to be closed off from the rest of the cast. Something Anastasia didn't anticipate, and for that... For that... She seemed to be eager to ignore Subaru's multiple deaths in such a small moment in favor of conversing and socializing with other people in the theater.

"... fufufu~" Priscilla's pleased chuckles filled the air at the misfortune of Anastasia, enjoying her misshape to a far greater degree.

"I-I'm not sure what any of this is, to be honest... I..." Kenichi found himself muttering without confidence anymore. He looked to his wife some more and found himself seeking some help. "I can't believe... It looked like the world was showing an intro to his 'adventure'... No? Naoko?"

The mother herself bit her lip and looked at their son. "It seemed that way..."

"He used to watch a lot of these Isekai stories back home before... Before he was taken..." Kenichi muttered some more into his hand as he moved it toward his chin.

"And he would read it too... That's what he told me once when he took too long at the convenience store." Noako held a small frown. "I would ask him not to spend time browsing and reading, or else he'd give the wrong idea to the seller and get them excited that he was going to buy something from them."

"Naoko..." Kenichi held on to his wife, giving her a look of sympathy as she trembled slightly at the memory. "It's okay... He is here right now."

"But we can't even talk to him!" Naoko sniffled while holding her son's other hand, the one not occupied by Emilia. "You saw how he was running through every death... You saw him... He was running and running as if the world didn't matter anymore! His deaths..." Naoko's face had the tears flowing shortly after as she kept holding on to her son. "How can he just run through his bodies like this?! Subaru! Please answer me... Please..."

Kenichi held on to his wife with a look of helplessness. Unable to say anything. Unable to do anything. Just a worthless man watching his family collapse.

Around them, there were eyes that couldn't continue watching the couple due to sheer emotion. Some, like Crusch and Rem, looked away out of respect.

"... Isekai stories..." Beatrice muttered, sitting back to reach Subaru's chest with the back of her head. Her eyes kept staring at the floor, thinking about the fact that she saw her mother on the screen and how much hurt Subaru will be going through. "Foreshadowing such a thing as you dying again... Subaru..." She whispered while trying to reach for either of his arms, but the sleeping boy had two ladies on his sides holding on to him.

Leaving Beatrice alone to just sit on his lap with her eyes empty. Brutally so.

"No matter how many times you die... huh..." Emilia sighed from the side, her eyes still glistening and staring at her boots. "You really are a dummy... You dunderhead... You lair..."

"Does it change your mindset now, Emilia-sama?" Rem asked from behind the half-elf and her knight, looking at her with pain clear in her eyes.

Emilia's gaze was surprised as she turned to Rem. "My mindset?"

"The one where you said you'd be accepting of what he's doing now." Rem's words were striking as Emilia's eyes flashed with the memories of Subaru's run through his corpses in the opening.

"Do you still agree to that way of doing things? To support this behavior from him?" Rem asked with her voice lower than usual, not wanting anyone else to be privy to what she was saying to the half-elf. Still... the maid had a tone of pleading in her voice.

One that wasn't lost on Emilia as the half-elf looked at the sleeve of Subaru's jacket with pursed lips.

It was only a moment, but Rem realized that Emilia didn't respond to her pleas for common sense because Emilia didn't have any refutation to give. The girl was merely contemplating as if she was a maiden presented with multiple strange cakes on her name day.

Rem couldn't help but grit her teeth as she remembered the same memories as Emilia did. How could she not? They had just watched Subaru's sacrificial nature in such a clear way that neither of them could deny it.

"Subaru must reeeeally be lonely... To feel like he can die over and over again for us..." Emilia's soft mumbles found their way to Rem's ears. And for a moment the maid held on to her breath.

"Yes." Rem sighed. Letting go of her skirt and ruffling it back to shape as her nerves gave out. "Yes... Subaru-kun feels lonely..."

"I won't let him be lonely after he wakes up... I promise..." Emilia's confident words were said without her trying to make eye contact with Rem.

And they didn't need to look at each other to agree on this one point. Whether Rem doesn't agree with Emilia or not.

Both girls could at least feel the same determination even through their different view points.

"I won't accept that he's hurting himself for me---for us. But... I agree that I won't let him have the chance to sacrifice himself either." Rem affirmed, taking to look at Subaru's head while crossing her legs as she leans back in her seat.

There didn't need to be more said between them. Whatever argument that Rem wanted to make had died when Emilia couldn't give a reaction to remembering Subaru's butchering other than glancing away.

"Crusch-sama?" Felix asked from the side by the time Emilia and Rem were talking to each other, earning him a small glare as the duchess had been trying to eavesdrop. The knight didn't flinch, however, as he began to speak unfettered. "Do you think you'll be alright to watch what happens next?"

At this, Crusch paused. A deep one that showed surprise on her usually reserved expression.

"What's the meaning of that statement, Ferris?" Crusch raised her eyebrow. "Have you become afraid of what comes next?" Though she sounded like she was teasing, Crusch wasn't the type to ask such a question lightly, as she gave her full sincerity behind the care she showed her knight.

"Ferri-chan would never feel scared when he's beside his lady, nya!" Felix couldn't help but let out a yelp of happiness with the way she overprotected his meager feelings. "Jeez... You really are remarkable, Crusch-sama."

"What...? I apologize, but I don't find anything I have done sufficient for such a compliment yet." Crusch sighed and turned her head away from Felix to glance toward Subaru.

Felix couldn't help but feel his heart ache slightly with the way she was focusing on the black-haired knight at the front.

He sighed and continued his question. "I was wondering when... When he left our protection after the White Whale was thwarted... Isn't it over for our role in the story, so to say?"

Crusch looked at her knight, her eyes showing thoughtfulness as she read between the lines expertly.

"You mean to say that we have no more part to play in Natsuki Subaru's story? I thought that would've been clear since what we are watching---"

"Why are we still watching, then?" Felix blurted out.

"... Are you suggesting that we might leave the viewings to the rest of the people here just because we aren't a party concerned by the coming events?" Crusch held him with a sudden firmness in her eyes.

"... I know that we care about him, Crusch-sama." Felix steadily walked this road with his face nervous yet brave. "But..." He let the moment pass so he could be sure to continue saying his piece. Oddly enough, Crusch was allowing him to do it with a patience he never expected. "But... What's the plan here? Do we just continue watching this depressing story even when it doesn't concern us anymore, nya?"

"Ferris, you disappoint me greatly." Crusch sounded baffled by her knight's audacity. "Has he not given up the most to help us achieve great peace across our world? Do we not owe him, as his allies and friends, to watch any more suffering he goes through?"

"But what if that ends up hurting us more than we can handle, Crusch-sama?" Felix responds with a bite in his lip, but Crusch would only glare at him.

"Speak clearly... What is it that you mean by this?" She demands of her knight.

Felix held her gaze with his own anxious one... His own protective one... His own scared one...

"After we watch what comes next... You'll be thrown by the wayside again, Crusch-sama..."

Her eyes widened due to his brutal honesty as he all but leaned forward to emphasize his words to her. "... Nya... I can't handle seeing you break or be forgotten yet again... And knowing him... He will be too busy fighting his damn hardest protecting those around him."

"Ferris" Crusch's tone silenced her trembling knight as she moved her gloved hand to weave her fingers through his hair. "You think far too much about the most ridiculous of things."

"..." He let her pat his head and sighed. Looking at the ground obediently.

"I refuse to be forgotten behind anyone in this room. Even if Natsuki Subaru would go on without me." Crusch grinned down at her knight with confidence that surprised him. "Do not fret, Ferris. I haven't come to be so pathetic that I'd be alright with such an ending for us."

"Us?" Felix looked at her in surprise. "Crusch-sama?"

"Of course." She held his head in the palm of her hand with care. Giving him a grin. "Who's going to take you with them in this room other than me? Priscilla?"

Felix shuddered at the memory of the last time he was alone with the red princess, leaning further into Crusch's palm and shuffling his head against it.

"Don't remind me of that wench, nya!"

Crusch gives in to her chuckles and ruffles his hair further. "Then we are stuck with one another, as always."

Felix's smile didn't reach his eyes as he looked up to his lady. "Yes, Crusch-sama... As always..."

"You are a cruel woman, Crusch-sama," Wilhelm murmured, his voice so low it barely carried over the soft sobbing of the Natsuki parents a few rows away.

From the seat directly beside them from Felix's side, Wilhelm watched the exchange with the practiced stillness of a man who had lived long enough to see the same tragedy play out in different costumes.

He saw the way Crusch's fingers moved through Felix's hair. To an outsider, it was a gesture of deep intimacy and comfort. To Wilhelm, it looked like a mercy killing.

"It is a heavy thing, is it not?"

Wilhelm glanced to his other side. Frederica was watching him, her demi-human eyes sharp and knowing. Beside her, little Petra sat like a statue, her small hands balled into fists so tight her skin looked like parchment.

The girl hadn't spoken since the 'opening' ended. She seemed to be shielding herself from the theater's atmosphere by simply refusing to participate in it.

"Devotion, Frederica-dono?" Wilhelm asked.

"The realization that devotion can become a cage," Frederica replied softly, her gaze flickering toward Rem, then back to Felix. "Crusch-sama knows that if she doesn't push him away now, he will simply wither in the shadow of whatever she becomes after this. She is being 'cruel' to give him a chance to survive without her."

"A bitter medicine," Wilhelm noted. "One I have seen many fail to swallow."

Wilhelm looked at Crusch some more. "She really is cruel."

He didn't mean it as an insult. In fact, there was a tinge of respect in his tired eyes. He saw exactly what she was doing. "Forcing Felix-dono to choose between sticking with his obsession with a woman who's heart is already set on Natsuki Subaru... Or choose to move on from his one true lady."

"Subaru-sama doesn't make it easy," Frederica added with a faint, sad smile. "He has a way of making everyone feel like they are the center of his world, even when he is dying for a hundred others at the same time. It makes people... possessive. Even the kind ones."

Petra didn't contribute. She just stared at the back of Subaru's head, her silence more deafening than any comment could have been.

Suddenly, a sharp snort erupted from the row in front of them. Felt was slumped in her seat, her boots kicked up on the armrest beside her. She didn't turn around, but her voice was loud enough to cut through the heavy philosophizing, yet low enough to be ignored by Felix and Crusch.

"Spirits, you people are depressing," Felt barked, her eyes still tired but defiant at the same time. "Is there a single person in this room who isn't busy tying themselves into knots over who loves who? Big Bro's out here getting shot down while running through a fuckin' tunnel of death and stabbed by psychos on a repeat, and you're back here analyzing the 'cruelty' of a headpat."

"Felt-sama, please," Reinhard began, his voice habitual and weary, but she cut him off with a sharp wave of her hand.

"No, 'please' nothing!" Felt finally turned her head, her red eyes locking onto Wilhelm with a fierce intensity. "Hey, Old Man. If you've got so much spare time to worry about Catboy and his lady problems, why don't you use those 'wise eyes' of yours to look about three feet to the left?"

Wilhelm stiffened. He knew exactly what, and who, she was referring to.

Felt gestured crudely toward Reinhard, who sat rigid, his gaze fixed forward, his expression a mask of perfect, agonizing composure.

"Talk to your grandson, Old Guy," Felt said, her tone losing its bite and becoming something closer to a command. "Before the next 'vision' starts and we all have to watch Big Bro die again. Ye're over here worried about some sad loser being 'forgotten,' while the guy who actually shares your blood is sitting right there, looking like he's already a ghost. Sort your own mess out."

Wilhelm opened his mouth to respond, to offer a dismissal, a platitude about the 'Astrea way', but the words died in his throat. He looked at Reinhard's back. The Sword Saint looked invincible, a pillar of strength. But from this angle, Wilhelm could see the slight tremor in his grandson's hands.

Encouraged by Felt's bluntness, or perhaps simply pushed to the edge by the weight of the White Whale's viewing being so long ago, Reinhard finally turned. He didn't look like the Kingdom's strongest asset, he looked like a boy trying to navigate a minefield.

"Grandfather," Reinhard began, his voice surprisingly steady despite the tension. "I... I wish to ask you something. Regarding the previous viewing."

Wilhelm didn't turn his head, but his eyes shifted toward the youth. "Speak."

"Your reaction to the elf... to Sphinx," Reinhard said, his blue eyes searching Wilhelm's hardened face. "I have never seen you lose your composure in such a way. Not even during the fight with the Whale. Why did her presence provoke such... such absolute hatred?"

The air around Wilhelm seemed to drop ten degrees. He finally turned his body to face Reinhard, his hands resting heavily on his knees.

"Sphinx was a monster of the Demi-human War, Reinhard-dono," Wilhelm said, his voice like grinding stones. "A biological filth that should have remained buried. She was the reason for more suffering than you can comprehend. And..." He paused, his gaze darkening. "She was a great terror to the woman who's meant to be the grandmother of that man."

He jerked his chin toward Roswaal, who sat further away, his expression unreadable behind his makeup.

"I demand answers," Wilhelm growled. "I want to know why Roswaal is housing a creature that bears the same face as that monster who'd slaughtered so many."

"Then why aren't you attacking him?" Felt's voice cut in again, sharp and impatient. She was leaning over the back of her seat now. "If you're so pissed off, Old Man, go over there and squeeze his neck until he talks. Why are we just sitting here?"

"Because he is protected," a new voice said.

It was Petra. The young girl didn't look up, her voice sounding hollow and devoid of its usual childish warmth. She talked, with her gaze fixed on the blank screen.

"When we were told about Warden-sama... Whoever took his place in charge of this place," Petra continued, her tone chillingly flat. "It protected Roswaal-sama when Garfiel-san tried to attack him earlier. It doesn't matter how much we hate him or what he's hiding. It's unfair..."

Felt snorted, but she didn't disagree. Instead, she turned her sights on Frederica. "And what about you? You're the one who gave Big Bro that letter, right? The one that sent him to this 'Sanctuary' place in the first place. Roswaal's got a plan, and you're the one who opened the door for him."

Frederica's expression didn't flicker. She stood straight, her hands clasped in front of her in a perfect maid's stance. "I performed my duties as instructed, Felt-sama. I have no information to give regarding my master's private affairs."

"Doubt that," Felt muttered dryly. "You're all the same. Secrets on top of secrets."

"There is more at play," Petra whispered, her voice almost ghostly. "Roswaal-sama isn't just being protected because he's a participant. He's being used too..."

"Petra, that is enough," Frederica said softly, shushing the girl with a firm but gentle hand on her shoulder.

Wilhelm looked back at Reinhard. He saw the conflict in his grandson's eyes, a desire to protect the fragile peace of the theater or seek justice and answers.

"The child is right, Reinhard," Wilhelm said, giving a grim nod toward Petra. "It is clear that none of us can get a clear answer while the entity holding this theater is actively shielding him. We are not audience members... we are prisoners of a story that hasn't finished being told."

Reinhard lowered his head, the weight of his own powerlessness in this room finally sinking in. "I understand, Grandfather."

"That fuckin' sucks." Felt huffed, eyeing Petra some more, but rolling her eyes to look somewhere else.

*CLICK*

The air in the theater suddenly vibrated with a low, bone-deep hum. The screen, which had been a dull, obsidian void, began to pulse with a faint light with a flow of imagery that kept playing at such a speed that no one could tell what each image was portraying.

The conversations that had been splintering into small, bitter groups died instantly as every head turned toward the display.

"Wait! We can't just... we shouldn't just start the next one yet!" Otto stood up, his hands waving frantically. His face was pale, his eyes darting between the screen and his own camp. "We haven't even processed what we just saw! There are too many things left unsaid, and if we keep watching without a plan, we're just going to---"

"What we 'should' do is get the truth," Julius interrupted, his voice cutting through Otto's panic like a rapier.

He stood as well, his posture perfect, but his hand was white-knuckled where it rested on his hip, not that anyone could see beneath his glove. "You speak of discussion, yet your camp remains a fortress of silence. I demand to know about this Witch of Greed and that person named Sphinx. If Roswaal L. Mathers is housing an entity that shares the face of a monster from the Great War, then this is no longer a private matter of the Emilia Camp. It is a threat to the kingdom."

Julius turned his gaze to Otto, his expression softening only slightly. "Otto. You are a man of reason. Trust me. Tell me what you and Garfiel are hiding. We are your allies, aren't we?"

"It's not that simple, Julius-san!" Otto cried out. "Garfiel's problem wasn't even about any witch present within the Sanctuary! It was---"

"It was about everythin'!" Garfiel snarled, almost leaping out of his chair. He didn't look at Julius, simply turned around to look at Roswaal in the back with a feral, murderous intensity. "I ain't gonna be askin' for pity or understandin' out 'ere! I blame that makeup-wearin' bastard for everythin' that's happenin' to Cap'n! He's the one who let all this rot happen!"

"And that's precisely what we need to understand!" Tivey piped up from the Anastasia camp, his monocle glinting in the screen's light. "The Emilia camp is hoarding information like dragons. You watched the intro, didn't you? We saw Natsuki Subaru die a dozen times in seconds before this even started. His safety is clearly at zero, and you're all standing around him like a wall of secrets. How can we help him if we don't know what he's fighting?"

The parents watched the chaos from their seats. Naoko didn't look at the arguing knights. Her eyes were fixed on Emilia, who looked smaller than ever, and then shifted to Rem.

The blue-haired maid looked utterly lost. Having been in a coma during the events being shown, she was witnessing the disintegration of her friends' trust in real-time.

Kenichi, however, felt a gaze on the back of his neck. He turned slightly to see Priscilla sitting in the very last row. She was lounging like a queen on a throne of boredom, a fan partially obscuring a sharp, knowing grin.

She caught his eye and gave a slow, deliberate wink.

The message was clear.

Look at them. See how they squabble while your son bleeds. My offer remains. Bring him to me, and he will be a king, not a martyr.

Kenichi's jaw tightened, but he looked away, his eyes landing on Crusch. Unlike the others, she was still. She sat with her chin resting on her hand, her eyes contemplative as she weighed the room's energy.

"Enough," Crusch said. It wasn't a shout, but the authority in her voice acted like a physical weight, forcing the room into a sudden, shaky silence.

She looked at Julius. "Julius Juukilius, stand down. Your pursuit of justice is noble, but your timing is flawed. If we wish for answers, they are literally inches away from manifesting on that screen. Why argue over fragments when the whole truth is about to be forced upon us?"

Julius hesitated, his eyes flickering toward his lady.

"Oh? My, Crusch-san~, you certainly sound authoritative today," Anastasia remarked, her tone playful but her eyes narrowing into cold slits. "But I don't recall givin' ya the right to command my Knight. Ya might wanna remember whose camp ye're talkin' to before ya start issuin' orders."

Crusch didn't even blink at the thinly veiled threat. She simply gave a dry ghost of a smile.

"I merely wanted to see if you were still alive over there, Anastasia-sama. You've been quiet for a minute too long while your knight disrupted my affairs, I worried the theater had claimed you."

Anastasia snorted, a sharp, un-ladylike sound. She waved a hand dismissively toward Julius. "Back down, Julius. She's right, even if she's bein' a bit of a nag about it. Let's see what the big show has for us next."

Julius bowed stiffly and took his seat, the tension in his shoulders not leaving for a second. Otto sank back into his chair, wiping sweat from his brow, while Garfiel continued to vibrate with suppressed rage.

"That was so funny," Meili giggled, her feet dangling off the edge of her seat. She looked at the screen, her eyes shimmering with a dark, childish glee. "I wonder if Onii-san can hear how much of a fuss people make over him while he sleeps."

She turned her head to Schult. "What do you think, Rosie-chan? Don't you think that they're annoying someone who's very scary~?"

Schult, sitting directly beside her, didn't giggle. He glared at the assassin girl, his small face contorted with a mixture of anger and a strange kind of pity. "You should be quiet, Meili-san. You shouldn't say such mean things when everyone is hurting."

Meili turned her head, her smile sharpening. "Oh? And why not? Onii-san looks so much more interesting when he's not acting like some hero, doesn't he? To keep going even after seeing a pile of your own corpses... it's almost like a game!"

"Liar," Schult snapped.

The sudden edge in his voice caused Meili's smile to falter for a split second. Schult leaned in closer, his voice low enough that only she could hear. "I was right here. When the music was playing and he was falling... I felt you shaking. You weren't laughing then... You were afraid of him."

Meili's expression hardened into a mask of mockery. She reached out and flicked Schult's forehead with a sharp clack. "You talk too much for a little puppy. Maybe I should sew your mouth shut so you can't tell any more stories."

Schult didn't flinch. He rubbed his forehead but kept his eyes locked on hers. "You can be as mean as you want. But if you keep hiding behind these lies, you're going to end up alone. You won't have any friends, Meili-san."

Meili opened her mouth to deliver a scathing retort, but her gaze inadvertently drifted down to the row below them.

There sat Petra, hunched over, her silence a stark contrast to Meili's loud cruelty. Meili's purple eyes lingered on the maid-in-training for a long moment, a flash of something unreadable, envy, or perhaps curiosity, crossing her face.

"Friends are for dummies," Meili whispered, though the bite was gone from her tone.

In the very back, away from the prying eyes of the candidates, Ram stood behind Roswaal. The Margrave's makeup was as immaculate as ever, but in the dim, pulsing light of the screen, he looked like a marionette with its strings tangled.

"Master," Ram said, her voice a calm anchor in the theater's chaotic sea. "What are you thinking?"

Roswaal tilted his head back, giving her a playful, exaggerated wink that didn't reach his mismatched eyes. "Thinking? Why, Ram, my d~eee~ar, I'm thinking that the lighting in this room is dreadfully unflattering to my complexion!"

"Stop it," Ram commanded. Her voice didn't rise, but it held a finality that silenced his theatrical hum. "What have you prepared for when the 'Warden' shows the rest? When every secret you've buried in that Sanctuary is brought to light? Do you have a plan?"

Roswaal looked at her. For a heartbeat, the mask slipped.

Ram didn't see a mastermind.

She saw a man who was utterly, completely helpless. He was a scholar who had lost his book, a wizard who had run out of spells. He had prepared for a world that followed a script, and the script had been burned before his eyes.

Not only that, but he gave a small, genuine shrug, his shoulders drooping.

Ram let out a long, weary sigh. She reached up and adjusted the collar of his coat, her touch lingering for a second longer than necessary.

"You really are a hopeless fool of a master," she murmured, her insult wrapped in a layer of fierce, unwavering devotion. "I suppose I'll just have to keep picking up your pieces, even when the world sees how broken you truly are."

Roswaal didn't respond. He simply looked up at the screen as the hum reached a crescendo.

The viewing had begun.

"My name is Echidna..."

At that, Kenichi's breath hitched. He was so focused on the danger that could be presented to his son that he forgot to see the change in his own boy.

He could see that his son was no longer trying to waste his body on useless movements to be cool or cautious. Subaru's eyes were scanning for exits even when he was in an open field with someone as mysterious as this Echidna.

The over-the-top energy Kenichi had spent years trying to instill in him had been replaced by a cold, sharpened edge.

"He's just being cautious..." Kenichi would whisper to his wife, hollow, without conviction.

The two held their hands together with Naoko putting more into her touch on Subaru's hand.

The knight merely gave a humble nod and flashed a waving hand towards Otto, canceling any violence from the merchant.

Garfiel didn't respond, even when Otto shook him though the merchant had no strength to actually move Garfiel's body when the blonde tenses his muscles. So Otto gave up on the idea of demanding physically, merely settling back in his seat.

Among the wave of the people still reacting, another person who was included within the boy's group but far away from them to be of any assistance to the increasing fracture of their friendship, Reinhard was observing the screen with increasing curiosity, though he more so observed the others's reactions rather than give his own.

She tried to ignore just how giddy Anastasia's suddenly became.

Crusch's eyes landed slightly on the blonde drills that could be noticed on Subaru due to the spirit using him as her seat.

The ones who should be comfortable seeing her on screen with Subaru.

A giggle that rang out through the field of grass as if nothing else existed with her voice.

"This has to be a joke... Don't tell me I actually feel excited to see her again with all her goofy acts trying to be human... Teacher..." Al sighed in his helmet, leaning down to stare at Echidna even more clearly through the slits.

"What is this place?" Subaru asked from the bottom of the hill. "I was in some dark ruins just a moment ago! When did you move me?!"

"Unfortunately, you're mistaken. You weren't physically moved anywhere." Echidna answers breezily from her chair beneath the umbrella. "You were merely invited to my tea party."

"Tea party?" Subaru had a curious expression on his face now.

Echidna closes her eyes and explains with a smile. "A witch's tea party."

"She seems nice," Schult commented anxiously, looking toward his lady when he heard her snort.

"Have more perception, Schult. That woman is no more deserving of our time than the dirt beneath mine divine heel."

"Priscilla-sama?" Schult tilted his head as Priscilla snorted, giving the witch on the screen a mocking grin.

"Tis nothing to concern your cute head about," She waved at him, cancelling his attention from her. "This divine merely found amusement at such a folly being played on the commoners of this world."

"Whatcha mean by that?" Heinkel asked as politely as he could, showing as much intrigue as Schult and Meili now as they heard what Priscilla had to say.

The confidence she gave as she judged Echidna before anything could be explained about the Witch was a spectacle to the two children and Heinkel.

But it was much more than that to the knight who'd turned his head to look at Priscilla with widened eyes.

"Don't you see how far she goes to act human? She's far more dependent on our world than we are led to believe... That Witch isn't worth your consideration for being nice or anything more, Schult."

"I see..." Schult shrugged and looked back at the screen, feeling safe to be there beside someone as confident as his lady.

"Something the matter, Aldebaran?" Priscilla would ask, glancing with her head tilted at the man next to her.

Al's quiet was almost broken then and there, as his helmet stared at the lady with something ominous and foreboding. An omen of sheer negativity as she grinned at him with her full confidence on display with her red irises shining in their beauty.

"Nothing, Princess." Al turned away from her with a hint of gratitude that couldn't be explained to those unaware of Al. Which seemed to be everyone in the theater as he was one of the most offish and calm characters inside the room.

Except for one more person other than Priscilla who truly does notice Al from time to time.

Ram looked away from the helmeted knight after seeing Priscilla wasn't giving him any trouble.

The screen zooms out to highlight the fact that Subaru was still at the bottom of the grassy hill while she sat in front of her tea set on top.

"Are you going to stand there forever?" She opens her arm for him to invite himself closer. "Why don't you at least sit down before the tea gets cold?"

Subaru seemed to hesitate for a second before he shook his head roughly. "All right, fine!" He stomped up the hill to reach the lady.

"Emilia-sama...?" Petra sounded from the back, pausing the screen and bringing attention on herself as well as the half-elf by everyone in the theater.

This was the first time Petra was showing an emotion which was determination or curiosity since the last viewing had ended.

"Yes, Petra?" Emilia gazed back at the little maid with soft eyes. "What's the matter?"

"... Did you meet her as well?" Petra gestured to Echidna on the screen. Finding eyes widening among the room.

"Why would Emilia-sama have met with the Witch?" Tivey asked with alarm.

Emilia seemed to have an easy time ignoring the attention on her after having been subject to this scenario far too many times. She merely held Ptra with her gaze and gave a small nod.

"I've met Echidna as well, yes... In my trials, though... She never offered me any tea or was this nice to me." Emilia muttered with little disappointment as she trailed back to the screen.

While everyone widened their eyes at the revelation from Emilia, gaining her curious looks from Crusch and the parents, Petra was the one to ask the next question.

"Is she... safe?"

"Safe?" Emilia tilted her head for an elaboration.

"Is she a bad guy...?"

Schult would straighten his back behind Petra's seat. Since he was the on closest in the back row, he was more invested due to what his lady was saying about Echidna a moment ago.

Emilia had the entire room other than Roswaal, Beatrice, and Al looking at her with baited breath.

"It's not... She can be mean? She's a lot more dishonest about her feelings than I'd like to consider healthy... But." Emilia trailed off for a moment, noticing that Beatrice was giving her a raised brow.

"But?" Naoko urged from the side, beside Emilia. "Speak up, honey. Please."

"But... She's got a good heart." Emilia's statement founded a sense of relief that ran across the room. Though another wave of disdain ran alongside the first one from select people.

"Yeah right... Bet anyone who's not a failed project of hers gets that feeling about her too." Al muttered softly, glaring away from Emilia's stupidity.

While Roswaal glared at her openly with disgust and distaste, clicking his teeth.

Beatrice meanwhile, had the most tame reaction among the Echidna squad of the cast. As a matter of fact...

The small spirit had a smile at Emilia's sincere words...

Before a second had passed from when he reached the top, he sat down and gulped a teacup that was already reserved for him by the Witch.

Said witch was looking at him with a blinking expression. As if befuddled by his behavior, he set the cup down with a loud 'clank.'

Al choked as soon as he saw her expression on the screen.

"... How could you not thank her for the honors, in fact?!" Beatrice could only shriek after blinking in complete astonishment as Ricardo and Felt let loose a laughter so loud they could immediately be heard falling from their seats.

"Is he a fool?" Crusch wondered to herself, looking at the floor for a second before shaking her head at Felix who actually was trying to answer that question. "Never mind." She palmed her face while Frederica held her hand to her mouth out of surprise.

Emilia and Rem both sighed at the same time, looking very much tired at the antics displayed by Subaru.

"You could ask what it contains before drinking? Maybe?!" Rem mumbled with a frustrated pout.

"Dunderhead..." Emilia whined into her hand as she hid her face in both palms.

"Onii-san's going to die from that isn't he?" Meili surprised Schult by sounding almost somber when saying that.

"We hope not..." Schult commented, giving her a small smile, thinking she was worried for Subaru.

"... That would be so lame! What a loser way to die!" Meili's expression of outrage and disappointment didn't hint that she was seeing the flaws in her logic. And so Schult merely turned away from her with a deadpanned face.

Garfiel was letting out a small scoff with a look of admiration on his face. "Cap'n's got them in metal and size..." Though the blonde didn't have the usual cheer he'd give for Subaru's idiotic tendencies.

Beside him, Otto just held on to his head from both sides, covering his ears and looking at the ceiling like a drowned patient with no soul in his stare which aimed at God himself. It was all he could do to convulse all his energy so he could stop himself from jumping and sending Subaru a fresh knuckle sandwich.

Behind them, though more quiet than he'd like to be, Julius felt his entire body collapse as he leaned back with the same stare of death aiming for a god in the ceiling of the room so he can pray to them and end his suffering.

"He just drank that...?" Anastasia was still blinking with amazement. "The stupid displayed right now must make ya doubt yer education of the boy, huh?" She couldn't help but tease the parents a bit, trying to add levity in the worst way possible.

Kenichi and Noako looked at Anastasia together with innocent expressions. "What are you talking about? Whose education?"

Anastasia blinked again but this time at the parents who stared back at her like befuddled cats. "The education of your son." She pointed at Subaru with her head, glaring at Kenichi a bit.

"I have no son." Kenichi's innocent expression didn't change while Naoko seemed not to understand anything but she trusted her husband so she imitated him like a cat.

Anastasia realized that Subaru was just disowned for being so stupid and she resulted to look up at the ceiling with her own stare towards God.

"You're awfully brave, gulping down tea served by a witch in one gulp."

"Hah! If you really wanted to kill me, I'd be a pile of ash the very next instant." Subaru smirked, though a bead of sweat could be seen running down his cheek.

"What's this tea, anyway? It's not bad, but it's not good, either." Subaru looked down at his cup curiously.

"It's something that's produced here." Echidna began to explain, "To put it bluntly, it's a body fluid of mine."

"WHAT THE HELL DID YOU JUST MAKE ME DRINK?!" Subaru shouted, standing up and slamming his hand on the table multiple times.

It was Petra's turn to look away to the far off wall and stare deadpanned for a god somewhere as the disgust overtook the room.

"EW!" Mimi blew her tongue out and shook her head as if she was the one who drank the tea on screen.

Reinhard and Wilhelm showed expressions of sympathy while the only person who found that disgusting fact funny were the duo of chaos Heinkel and Meili as they cackled for their own reasons due to Subaru's misfortune.

"She really can't let anyone forget..." Al sighed while looking away, not to any god this time, just tired already.

Subaru's parents both looked back to Anastasia, having their innocent expressions to intensify what Kenichi had said from before, which Anastasia glared at them for in the middle of her expression being turned to one of disgust.

"All of a sudden, I'm veeeery glad not to have been invited for tea by her..." Emilia muttered into her sleeve, holding on to Subaru's hand in pity for him.

"Subaru-kun must not drink any bodily fluids without prior notice!" Rem huffed with a small blush on her cheeks.

"I think... You need to be more disgusted than outraged by the optics there... Rem..." Crusch blinked at the maid's skewed sense of priority.

"Retribution for Barusu's stupidity is surely fulfilling to watch," Ram was highly pleased that Subaru got consequences this disgusting for his recklessness.

All the Witch did in reply was giggle like a mischievous schoolgirl.

"You truly are a fascinating individual." Echidna held a dark gaze that was laid-back but interested, amused, et calculating. "You're standing right here in front of me as if it's normal."

Subaru had to give a small chuckle at that.

"Hah! You think you're so pretty that a normal person's eyes would fall out? I'll have you know that my eyes are blessed throughout the day by the prettiest girl alive! So no matter how many times I look at you, I'm not going to see you as particularly pretty."

"Subaru..." Emilia let out a cute squeak, feeling her cheeks redden so she covered her face by letting her silver hair fall down and leaned away from everyone. She had a kind smile and found it easier to meet Naoko's eyes.

The mother held a small smile for the half-elf as well, seeing a bit more clearly now that her son was speaking about his crush in front of another woman. "I never thought I'd hear him say that about a girl before my hair went gray..."

"Guess we're lucky..." Kenichi found a bit of dark levity in their situation as he grinned at his wife. "We get to see it in full HD, babe."

Naoko sighed, not at all willing to laugh at that but still willing to lean into her husband some more. "At least he's as terrible at complimenting girls as his old man." She teased softly while her husband flexed his shoulder muscles so she could lean on him more.

"That he is!"

While the parents were having their small moment, Rem was behind everyone looking away from the screen with a little eye roll. Surprising Crusch by showing a more openly jealous attitude.

"It's never easy to hear your crush say that about another woman, Crusch-sama." Felix noted with a hint of worry for his lady, watching her carefully.

Crusch shrugged and patted Rem's shoulder before looking back at the screen, not at all affected.

"Aren't you... going to be sad that he's not complimenting you?" Felix asked which made the duchess raise her brow.

"Why would I? He's already told me that he finds me suitable for the eyes when he was in our care." Crusch's reply was calm and logical. Not at all affectionate or emotional like Felix wanted it to be.

"N-Nevermind then... Nya..."

Echidna didn't even react to such an assault on her beauty, merely smiling at Subaru innocently. "No, most normal people vomit when they stand before me. Funny, right?"

Subaru's hands slam on the table as he glares at her. "There's nothing funny about that!"

But she only laughs in sheer delight.

"She really never smiled or giggled like this with me..." Emilia found herself looking more curiously at the Witch. "Does she like Subaru already?"

"She's just a construct, I suppose." Beatrice sighed, though her eyes seemed hopeful as well, like a cat that's missing affection. "I never thought I'd see Mother giggle like a maiden again."

"What a nasty fucking laugh, Teacher." Al commented dryly from the back though he didn't let anyone hear him. "Trying to play your part well... Or maybe Pal really does produce such human feelings from you... Hm..."

While the knight was contemplating, Roswaal was all but glaring at the big screen with as much hatred as he possibly could.

"What is it you want with me?" Subaru glares at her with more scrutiny.

"Nothing really," Echidna's smile relaxes to reveal more of her calculating expression of interest. "You came in here of your own accord. If you want to go back, I'll send you back..."

She gains a dangerous white gleam inside of her abyss-like irises, "But are you sure that's what you want?"

"An opportunity to talk to the Witch of Greed is not something that just anyone, other than you, can earn." Her voice was smooth in delivery, perfect on the ears as she spoke with honeyed wording.

"Everytime she says that title as if she owns it... A vein within me ruptures..." Roswaal spits, moving to turn his head away and uncrossing his legs for the first time since the previous viewing, Ram wondered if he ever felt numb sitting like that.

"You truly do despise her... Does she act like your Teacher?" Ram tilted her head, curious.

Roswaal bristles at the very notion. "Never assume such a stupid thing again," he hisses hatefully. "My Teacher is far beyond the need to act this animated to manipulate others into thinking she's human!"

"So Barusu's charm's not to blame for how 'favorable' she's trying to be to him?" Ram sighed and turned from her agitated master.

"Not at all... Subaru-kun is very much deserving of her attention even if she's an algorithm construct imitating my Teacher's soul." Roswaal let go of his hateful expression and sighed, going back to leaning back in his seat. "I didn't... Expect the construct to be so comfortable however..."

"You never got the chance to meet with the replica of your teacher's soul..." Ram noted without emotion, knowing how much that burns and stabs at the man's heart.

She could only look away and hum to herself, smiling slightly when he selfishly put a trembling hand on hers.

"The only requirement to have a dialogue is for both of us to exist. Let's remove everything unnecessary from the picture." Echidna's grin grew at the edges as the table, umbrella, teacups, and pot all erased themselves from existence.

Soon, the grass field around them both also began to disappear, eating itself to become vast emptiness and slowly racing towards Subaru and the Witch.

"Let me affirm all the desires, the curiosities, the greed, that you seek to satisfy with answers."

Above them, the cloudy blue skies began to crack in ugly deformation as if they were arctic ice swimming through the ocean. Only the ocean waters were a dark, ugly combination of rainbow colors instead of the sweet blue.

Subaru and Echidna remained in the middle of a world that was eating itself and a sky that was breaking like glass. Staring at one another head-to-head until the world around them turned into a painting out of cosmic fantasy.

Colors of the aurora borealis mixed with the magnificent lights of a thousand stars replaced the ruined fantasy world around them both.

Kenichi gripped the arms of his chair so hard the material groaned. He had seen planetariums in Japan, and he had seen the clear night sky on camping trips, but this... this was like staring into the eye of God.

"It's pretty..." Naoko whispered next to her husband, making him unable to do anything but look along at such a sight.

"The mana needed to conduct such an alteration of reality..." Reinhard muttered into his gloved hand, looking beyond impressed by the woman on the screen.

"... That's a witch for you." Julius glared at the colors on the screen, remembering his own spirits. "How could someone with such magical abilities escape the records---"

"Fuck the records," Heinkel snorted from above the knights. "Can't ya see that whenever this kid deals with shit like this, he'll always have otherworldly shit surrounding 'em without fault?"

"What a thing to hear from the so called deputy commander of the royal guard, nya..." Felix shook his head but noticed that Crusch was eyeing Heinkel with a certain tinge of respect.

"He's correct in that Subaru would be the only one to have someone capable of this much power meet him within just months of his stay in our world." Crusch analyzed the void around Subaru and Echidna with a hint of awe clear on her usually steeled expression. "How beautiful..."

"Now, what would you like to ask me?" Echidna spoke with confidence and self-satisfaction as Subaru gaped at her inside this dark, yet beautiful, world.

"... Could ya imagine the sheer knowledge someone like this has?" Anastasia mused with clear interest in her widened eyes. Her hands were both placed upon the blue scarf that's never left her neck since the start of the theater's imprisonment of their souls.

"Who'd waste such a grand prize as this?"

"M-My lady... You're sounding a little bit unhinged." Hetaro commented from the side, showcasing worry.

"He's right, you don't look natural, Anastasia-sama. Is everything okay?" Tivey affirmed his brother's worry with his own analytical criticism. "How come you're not worrying about Natsuki-sama?"

"Oh hush," Anastasia waved a hand at the twins. "Ya both need t' learn somethin' from yer sister. There's nothing like neglect happenin'."

"Who said anything about neglect?" Tivey's eyes narrowed at his lady, which earned him a couple of giggles from Anastasia.

Clearly, she wasn't taking him, or the situation itself any seriously than she felt the need to.

"I can't help but feel a bit uneasy about all of this," Hetaro commented with his brother nodding along.

"Oi, let's just watch what's happenin', eh?" Ricardo patted both boys on their heads, winking towards his lady and looking back up. "Boss' got a plan for the guy. Let 'er enjoy this."

"About the Witch of Gluttony, Daphne," she begins to explain, as a large dark cloud swims past Subaru's head, forcing him to look up as it forms shapes of unknown origins above him.

"Who created beasts that defied the will of God to save the world from starvation?" A black host of yellow eyes showed itself, but the screen moved to show Echidna's face, smiling in the colorful abyss as she continued.

"Created...? Huh?" Julius, who'd been noting down things since the beginning on a small notebook he'd borrowed from Tivey, suddenly blinked out of his trance of writing to look at Reinhard.

"But..." Reinhard found himself in awe with the notion, afraid to even speak it.

"I thought the Witch of Envy made the Mabeasts roam around to hurt us." Schult mumbled while looking around.

"Whaaaat? She's the mama of all my beasties?" Meili smirked in delight as she leaned forward. "Finally, this gets exciting!"

"Why is this information suddenly being given to Natsuki-san so freely?" Otto immediately frowned. "How come you don't share any of this information with the world outside the Margrave-san's territories?"

The closest Otto could ask was the girl sitting on Subaru's lap, which forced Subaru's parents to be privy to this conversation as they were sitting between them.

"Mabeasts... Was there supposed to be another witch that made them such things?" Kenichi asked politely, not knowing what was happening or why they were all focusing on another witch than Echidna.

"It's improbable for you to blame Betty for anything regarding the flow of information when she's been guarding the greatest library of knowledge for far longer than your ancestors were conceived, in fact." Beatrice crossed her little arms and glared back at Otto, fully taking offense.

Kenichi of course, was ignored, and he couldn't help but feel a little bit of an insult at that but a small pat on his back surprised him.

He glanced back and saw the large beastman, Ricardo, giving him a thumbs up and a grin. A notion of camaraderie which wasn't unwelcome by any means. Kenichi seemed to respect this person more every time they interacted.

"About the Witch of Lust, Carmilla, who granted emotions to non-human beings in an attempt to fill the world with love?" The shadow weaved itself throughout the abyss to form the image of a silhouetted figure with a large scarf flying behind their back.

"That sounds so... Sad..." Naoko commented with her eyes showing immediate sympathy for the woman.

"They don't sound like witches at all..." Emilia whispered under her breath, receiving several nods of agreement from around her.

"What a kind heart..." Rem mumbled, biting her lip as she looked at the scarf of the so called witch of Lust with sympathy. "I hope she didn't regret such a decision."
"About the Witch of Wrath, Minerva, who struck people to heal them as she lamented the state of her war-torn world?" The shadow moved to become the shape of a heroic woman with a ponytail that flew around the dark abyss.

Emilia's eyes widened in recognition with her smile being hidden behind her hair and hand. "Ah... That's who you were..."

While she was recognizing Minerva, the others reacted appropriately to what was being revealed in such tiny margins.

"I... Have never heard of such a being or Witch ever... That isn't even worthy of the title..." Julius looked down at what he was noting neatly upon his paper. "I find myself in more confusion than anything as to why these women are being condemned."

"Healing the world... By striking it?" Petra looked more interested by the tale being brought to her attention than anyone else in the room. "That... It sounds like a wonderful thing... Why would she be a witch for that?"

Crusch felt her arm being given a gentle squeeze, looking to her side, she saw that Felix was all by glaring at the screen.

"Does the concept of healing the world anger you, Ferris?" She would ask her knight with a raised brow.

The greatest healer in the kingdom shook his head. "No one would disagree with the concept... I just... Ferri-chan doesn't think it's fair to call someone a witch for healing... It's stupid, nya."

Her smile was small but clearly proud as she patted his head in affirmation of his frustrations.

"About the Witch of Sloth, Sekhmet, who drove a dragon past the great waterfall just for a chance to rest?" The shadow formed a woman who was lying down with a sinister glow within the area where the eyes would've been, had this not been a black shadow forming shapes and monsters.

"Damn," Ricardo blew a whistle as people around the room widened their eyes at that revelation.

"A dragon? Not a ground one?" Kenichi asked, looking at Otto since he was the closest surprised person to him and Naoko.

"Dragons that fly are a lot more prideful and chaotic... They are ancient beasts with no power to tame them." Otto explained to the parents with his appealing charm, understanding in his eyes that they would need such history for they are not of this world.

"What's the great waterfall?" Naoko wanders from Kenichi's side. "This feels nice to learn. It really does feel like we are watching a woman teach the history of the world."

"Too much history to be honest," Tivey muttered with his eyes closing in tiredness from how much he'd widened them the past minute alone.

"The great waterfall is the edge of our realm, I suppose." Beatrice answered her from the side, causing Otto to frown a bit. "Tis where your world would be... At least according to theory."

"Our world? Your 'realm' is just flat earth then?" Kenichi had a baffled face but a look of intrigue.

"Yours isn't like that, Natsuki-san?" Otto followed up with his own curious gaze, by now the screen had paused to follow the conversation since everyone was interested.

"Subaru never told us about this stuff... Well... Other than Beako." Emilia tried to say kindly, leaning a little bit over Subaru so she could hear the parents.

"Hmph! Try not to sound too jealous, in fact." Beatrice didn't try to stop the smugness in her tone.

Emilia pouted and pulled on a drill from the spirit, something she wouldn't usually do but she felt comfortable enough to do it today.

While Beatrice was yelping cutely on Subaru's lap, Kenichi shook his head with a smile. "He really doesn't take the chance to show his academic knowledge about anything, that boy of mine."

Anastasia couldn't help but affirm with a small hum and a grin while the father sighed.

"Well... Our world is essentially a sphere. Like a round globe of sorts that is covered by a large field. We have a saying that says 'Going 'round the world'? Something like that explains it." Kenichi watched every face in the room turn more interested and intrigued as if he was giving them prophetic knowledge. "Don't start a religion around this!"

"We wouldn't do such nonsense," Crusch chuckled. "Still, this is really fascinating." She received nods, even Priscilla was looking down at the parents with a raised eyebrow.

"I had thought it a bore to hear of past dead wenches but it seems like this led to more amusement, hm." The red princess noted with a pleasant expression. Like she was being entertained by the knowledge being given to her.

"Kenichi... Do you think..." Naoko held on to her husband's sleeve and pulled at it with a look of sudden realization.

Obviously all eyes were on her, including her husband's.

"Do you think um... That the dinosaurs and dragons in our world..." Naoko gestured towards the sleeping ground dragon in the room, as if to provide proof of what she was saying.

"Huh... No way... Oh my god!" Kenichi blinked as he thrust his chest forward to squint at Patrasche with more scrutiny.

"What is a dinosaur?" Meili tilted her head, receiving a glare from Petra. "What? I'm not finding any reason to stay quiet and I'm not insulting anyone so calm down!" She blew her tongue towards the glare of anger from her ex-friend.

"It's an old reptile that became extinct within the borders, or shall I say, rounded curves of their world, in fact." Beatrice explained with a smug smile, loving that she could hear the sigh from Emilia behind her. "Betty's Subaru told her they have a large similarity to the ground dragons in ours."

"They became extinct? How come?" Reinhard found himself more interested than ever.

"Are ya feelin' pity for 'em?" Felt raised an eyebrow, lazing against her chair but feeling like she wants to tease him.

"... A little. It's a sad thing to hear about the extermination of something like an entire race." Reinhard muttered.

"Ain't ya involved with exterminating various clans and races of demihumans should the kingdom of Lugunica deem it necessary like the Vollachian dickheads?" Heinkel, ever the dutiful son hater that he was, proceeded to screw over Reinhard's noble expression and make him sputter.

"Why did you say that so formally as if you're not a deputy commander of the same army that would help carry out such orders?" Al, ever the hater of Heinkel himself, couldn't stop himself.

"Gotta make 'em know what countries do what." Heinkel shrugged while nodding his chin towards the parents.

Schult gave him a small grin, seeing him being thoughtful for Subaru's parents instilled a feeling of great happiness in the small boy.

"Natsuki-san," Otto chuckled a bit. "Everyone's clearly interested in what you're thinking, so please don't keep us waiting."

Kenichi stopped his silence and looked away from Patrasche. "Well... Um... We don't really know how they became extinct... It happened long before our time in the world."

Beatrice kicked at Subaru's chin with the back of her heel as she dangled on his lap. "Betty was going to explain that, I suppose!"

"Sorry! But..." Kenichi held a small chuckle in his throat. "Sorry... Yeah. Well... It may be that your theory about what's beyond your waterfall could be correct."

"How come?" Julius found himself too interested not to urge from behind the parents. His pen never stopped.

"Well," Kenichi looked back at Patrasche and over at the scene paused on the screen above the entire room. "It could be that our dinosaurs and dragons came from your world... But that would imply Subaru's the first person to get to your world himself and... I don't know how that would happen since ours isn't flat."

Many in the audience had wonder and intrigue on their faces at the idea.

"Could be that we had your dragons and 'dinosaurs'... The opposite idea could be correct as well." Crusch mumbled into her glove, her eyes stretching with fascination and astonishment.

"Such a great deal for speculation." Roswaal sighed while glancing away from the room. He was the only one not interested in the talk, feeling a bit of dread for what might come next in the viewing but preparing himself.

"But that would imply a lot of these dinosaurs were moved into your world from ours..." Kenichi muttered, looking at his wife. "We don't even know why Subaru was the only human who got transported---"

"He isn't," Rem whispered while glancing at Subaru. "He's not alone in being taken from your... home."

Kenichi and Naoko both looked like a train was speeding toward them in a long tunnel.

"That's true..." Otto's eyes softened as he looked back. "We have forgotten about you for a while there, mostly because you said you didn't remember much, Al-san."

The parents looked back where Otto, and now seemingly the entire theater around them, had turned.

Towards the man wearing a helmet.

"Suddenly having all this attention on me makes me feel like I should've brought something better to wear... What am I saying, I always look my best." Al chuckled in his helmet but waved down at the parents. "Hey, Pal-san's folks! Nice to formally meet ya."

"You..." Kenichi swallowed, not helping the shocked undertone he was carrying. "You were taken from your home too?"

"Yeah... I was a bit younger than pal over there so I had... Lost more memories about my original home." Al didn't have a softer tone speaking this. His was the air of nonchalance and humility.

"Sweetie..." Naoko didn't know why she was compelled to say that but she held a hand to her heart. "Are you alright?"

It was clear to all the emotion on the mother's face wasn't for the missing arm.

Al lets out a chuckle as he usually does. "It's fine, Mother-san." He says easily without a hint of what he truly feels beneath the helm. All he can do is speak words that have no real connection with his heart. "I'm more than fine with how things are right now. I have a princess... And I... I don't want to remember what I'm missing from home. You're both lucky to see Pal before he ends up like me."

"Subaru... Forgetting where he came from..." Emilia muttered with her eyes glistening a bit at the sadness of such a thought. "I didn't know how grim it must be, Al-san..."

"Don't worry about me." Al's voice repeated over the room. It was a tension that was special to him due to the slight hint of emotion in his voice. The chaos ending his sentence with the tremble couldn't break through enough to their ears. It was either an echo because of his helmet... Or he was sobbing by the end of his sentence.

Either way... The parents looked over at the man with a hint of understanding, respect, and more so... A hint of relief.

"Al-san," Naoko gave a sad little smile towards the man, "if it would be alright with you and your lady," Priscilla raised an eyebrow at the respectful nod toward her. "I wouldn't mind having you for tea... or dinner? Or perhaps just for a walk... For when Subaru wakes up and we figure things out."

"That's a fantastic idea," Kenichi chuckled and looked at Al with a grin. "I see you speak Japanese, or at least the language that sounds Japanese everyone is speaking here. Which prefecture if you remember where you're from?"

"Saitama... " Al muttered.

The parents looked far more pleased than they wanted to be. "That's where we are living now," Kenichi grinned more excitedly.

"Yeah... It's where my parents are from as well." Al's voice was surprisingly cold, his hand was limp without an indication of movement. "You really want to have me over sometime?"

"Of course... You're always welcome at my household." Kenichi extended his own invitation as he held on to Naoko's shoulder, smiling up to the man hiding behind his helmet.

Al couldn't stop the slight stammer in his voice. "I... Uh... Sure... If Princess allows it... Thanks."

Kenichi grinned at Priscilla with a twinkle in his eyes, feeling happy and confident with the sudden camaraderie between Al and his own son.

Priscilla herself simply offered the man an annoyed raise of her eyebrow, though she didn't seem to reject or go against the parents or their initiative to involve her knight in their lives some more. Though one thing Priscilla appreciated was the polite remembrance of her ownership over Al.

"Hm... Commoners with manners."

"This has been very touching..." Julius gave Al a slight nod of respect, hoping the man got some closure with this new friendship Kenichi and Naoko were offering him. "But I would like to continue watching what Subaru's talk with the witch entailed for him."

A small wave of agreement sounded as Otto nodded his head to the parents, telling them they would talk about this later some more. Emilia gave Al another smile from her heart, hoping he would be better while turning around, holding on to Subaru's hand.

It was only Crusch that held on to a frown when looking at Al. Her eyes squinted slightly as she turned back to the screen and gripped her arm rest.

She didn't know if Reinhard was activating his own divine protection of the Wind Reading. But hers was always on.

And the wind around Al was nothing short of chaotic.

He was lying to them. He was lying to all of them about something... And Crusch was the only one who knew.

She looked at them. Seeing how Kenichi was holding on to his wife with a small smile of comfort and even encouragement.

The duchess closed her eyes and let out a tired breath. She didn't want to take this away from the parents who'd been constantly pushed to the side within the theater because of their isolation as other worldly guests.

It was one comfort that she can't be cruel enough to take away from them.

So Crusch... Stayed silent. And let Al be free of her intense scrutiny for the moment.

The screen played the next scene with its light breaking over the black helmet on Al's head as he sat in the quiet with nothing to say to anyone.

Even when Heinkel snorted and grunted in annoyance, "Fuckin' immigrant from another world, shoulda known."

And Schult was looking at him with something akin to awe and amazement.

But Al didn't focus on any of it.

He merely looked up to see Echidna's face. He felt a thousand emotions that tore through him at seeing her face. Likewise, he looked down back at Subaru's parents, feeling a million more emotions tearing him apart. Then he looked at Subaru... And he found only one emotion that tore through his heart and soul with one stab.

"Princess...? May I please ask for something?" Al turned to the only person he could confide in at this moment.

"..." Priscilla didn't interrupt him, she didn't even acknowledge him as she moved a hand towards his right hand. The one that wasn't missing.

"Those mean nothing when your divine is awaiting your determination to win, Aldebaran." Priscilla held a small grin as her hand slithered into his and kept him in a firm lock that squeezed gently, softly, and warmly.

Al... squeezed back as his eyes rolled up to the screen, feeling none of the thousand emotions because of Echidna's face that he did before.

Watching through the tears that streamed down his face inside his helmet.

But Echidna would continue. "About the Witch of Pride, Typhon, who, out of youthful innocence and cruelty, judged criminals one after another?" The shadow formed the form of a small, innocent girl, paused in a twirl forever.

"Aren't I a youth that's been makin' yer son kneel for my judgment for a while?" Felt raised an eyebrow towards Wilhelm with skepticism.

The old man didn't have a retort to say to that as he was left to look at the wall with a deadpanned stare, realizing the faultiness of his logic.

"My apologies."

Reinhard and Heinkel couldn't help the snorts that had to be hidden behind gloved hands.

And as the camera zoomed in on Subaru's trembling face, it clearly showed how disturbed he's become.

"About the Witch of Greed, Echidna," she continued without pause, "the embodiment of the thirst for knowledge, who has done things she regrets even in the realm of death, all in pursuit of all types of knowledge?" The screen zooms from Subaru's horrified expression to Echidna's much more sinister one, darkness covering her eyelids.

Subaru gasps as her words begin to take effect on him.

Al couldn't help but turn his eyes to Roswaal with his tears continuing to stream. Without the helmet, Al would've been able to show how unblinking his eyes were as they gazed upon Roswaal.

The hand on his squeezed tighter as his lady nudged him to turn back to the screen.

Fury because Al agreed with Roswaal's statement.

Subaru's face contorted into something inhuman, filled with despair and terror as flashes of the pictures from the storybooks he's read about the Witch began playing in his mind, finalizing to show her confrontation between her and Petelguese.

The warm and even friendly atmosphere became null with the little scenes that captured Subaru's terror displayed before everyone.

The parents themselves winced at the terrifying description given to them for the first time.

Naoko looked towards Emilia as her husband's question hung in the air.

The half-elf seemed to increase the grip on her son's arm as the light of the theater's screen shone across her silver hair over and over with every flash of the Witch of Envy bringing more trembles through Emilia's body.

She looked like a girl possessed at the mention of the Witch alone.

Kenichi's eyes widened even more, and his expression showed heartbreak as he looked down at his son.

With a snap, the dark world of abyss and colors cracked out of existence into nothing, replaced with the earlier grass field and blue, cloudy skies.

"Oh, darn." Echidna's tone suddenly returns to its innocent, girlish one. "It looks like I've frightened you too much."

Subaru seemed to be shaking in place.

Meanwhile, behind them, Rem moved a little closer to Subaru's seat, having a small frown of her own as she saw Petelguese's face in those flashes for a moment.

Roswaal didn't give her any disagreement. He merely looked away from the parents and Subaru to stare at the wall. Anything but watch this Echidna speak of herself as a witch.

She couldn't help but be curious when she saw the green-skinned man who faced Subaru and the Witch alone.

"Onii-san really has been up to no good before we met... Hm..." Meili couldn't help but pout as she kicked her feet to dangle them again and again. "Wish I could've seen that..."

And immediately his face began twisting in a pained expression, holding on to his stomach.

Subaru seemed to step back because of this, a confused expression replacing his pained one. "Huh?"

I feel like...

"Please," Otto's voice came out as a strangled whimper. "Just... let the screen go black again. I don't want to see this."

"You look like a bug that got stepped on, Mister Merchant," a high-pitched, giggling voice drifted down from the row above.

Meili was leaning over the back of her chair, her chin resting on her crossed arms. She poked a finger toward the screen, tracing the "swirls" in Otto's eyes with a dark, childish glee. "Does your head feel like it's full of bees? Onii-san looks so surprised to see you like this. I wonder if you'll make a funny sound if he kicks you."

"Meili! That's horrible!" Petra snapped, though she too was looking at the screen with intense worry.

"It's just a question," Meili hummed, her purple eyes shimmering with a mischievous, misunderstood coldness. "When things get scuffed up, they usually break. He's like a very sturdy weed."

While the girls bickered, a different kind of tension was radiating from the blonde youth sitting near the front. Garfiel was vibrating. Not with anger, but with a raw, jagged dread that made his fangs click together.

This is it, Garfiel thought, his claws digging into the plush armrests. This is where Cap'n and I meet...

He looked at the screen, at the ruin of the merchant he'd just finished "questioning", and then toward Subaru's sleeping face. The fear of being rejected by the only man he called "Captain" was a weight heavier than any stone in the Sanctuary.

"Garfiel?" Frederica whispered, reaching out a hand to her brother. He flinched away, his gaze locked on the display.

"Shut it," Garfiel growled, though his voice lacked any bite. It was the sound of a boy waiting for a sentence to be passed.

"It looks like he's been through a tumble dryer," Kenichi noted, trying to inject some levity into the room, though his eyes were sharp. "He looks... well, he looks like he met a wall. A very fast-moving wall."

"It was no wall, Natsuki-san," Julius said, his eyes narrowing as he looked from the screen to Garfiel's rigid back. "That is the work of a predator. Someone wasn't happy about Subaru entering those ruins."

"Otto?!" Subaru exclaimed before another gruff voice cut in.

"Look at you, just waltzing out of there! You've got some nerve, outsider!" The voice was filled with loud and obnoxious revelry as Subaru was suddenly held up by someone who had snuck behind him.

"Is this like a magmarin laughing when it's full of holes?!" Sharp teeth are shown, owned by the person holding Subaru up like a sack of potatoes, before he was sent to fly high in the sky by a mere throw.

"SUBARU!" Naoko gripped her seat.

"Oh, look at him fly!" Meili giggled, her eyes wide with genuine amusement. "Do you think he'll bounce, or just go 'splat' like a overripe melon? I always liked the 'splat' sound best."

"Meili, stop it!" Petra cried, but the assassin girl only leaned further forward, watching the trajectory with clinical interest.

Subaru screamed through the air in the manliest way possible from the great MC of this story. So manly, in fact, that it summoned his ground dragoness from her great slumber to screech and run with the carriage attached to her, following suit.

Patrasche did a sharp turn with her body moving in such a way as to force the carriage to physically drift in the dirt and align itself in a way that Subaru's fall is broken by the ceiling of the fancy cart before he falls back down on the leathered seating for the drivers.

As Subaru was getting his bearings together from his fall, Patrasche faced the sharp-toothed person who dared to commit such a heretical policy of violence on her rider.

"I don't... I don't understand," Emilia whispered, her voice trembling. She looked back at the screen, her brow furrowed in a deep, agonizing confusion. "I was with Subaru. We went into the woods together. I should have been right there when he came out... but I don't remember this."

"YES!" Kenichi suddenly stood up, his fist pumping the air with a roar of triumph that startled everyone. "THAT'S MY GIRL! Did you see that?! That was a professional-grade catch! Patrasche-chan, you miracle of nature!"

He didn't just look at the screen, he turned around to look at the physical Patrasche, who was still curled up in a corner of the theater, her chest rising and falling in deep, unconscious breaths. Kenichi rushed over to the sleeping dragoness, regardless of the tension in the room, and began doting on her with abandon.

"Who's the best girl? Who's the smartest, most heroic lizard in two worlds? You are!" Kenichi cooed, patting her snout and adjusting her harness with the pride of a man whose daughter had just won a championship. "Look at her, Naoko! Subaru doesn't deserve her, the lucky brat!"

"She really is... remarkable," Crusch murmured, a hint of professional envy in her eyes as she watched the dragoness on screen immediately square up against Garfiel.

"Damn it, what the hell?!" Subaru said in a pained growl, sitting straighter on the driver's seat of the cart.

He quickly turns around to open the driver's window and lean into the carriage to see inside. He finds Emilia's sleeping form inside, perfectly placed straight on the carriage couch.

"I was asleep?!" Emilia widened her eyes in remembrance and outrage. "Oh... Oh that's when they met..." She looked to Garfiel with pity.

Subaru breathed out in relief, gaining a small smile, before the loud-voiced attacker started speaking.

"What a tantalizing choice!"

Subaru stands up on the carriage driver's spot, glaring alongside Patrasche towards their attacker.

The blonde man with an X scar on his face grins, standing tall. "That's a nice ground dragon ya got there."

Garfiel, in the theater, couldn't look away. He saw himself on the screen, the predator, the bully, and he saw the dragoness who had more loyalty in her pinky claw than he had in his entire body at that moment.

"I'm sorry, Cap'n," Garfiel whispered into his hands, the guilt finally breaking through his facade as the screen focused on Patrasche's bared teeth and Subaru's trembling, grateful gaze.

Patrasche screeched at the blonde man and began to charge toward him while Subaru immediately held on to the carriage with increasing panic. "Wait, Patrasche!"

But the dragoness had somehow disconnected herself from the carriage, so Subaru now watched her dash to attack the strangely calm man on her own.

Both Subaru and Otto looked on with bated breath as Patrasche moved to bite the mere human who had caused her rider such distress.

But when she bit on his shoulder, the dragoness couldn't even make the blonde man flinch as he stood in place without a problem to allowing the dragon's mouth to bite into his upper shoulder.

"You're a real good-lookin' girl, ain't ya? I'm not gonna hurt ya!" The blonde wrapped his arm around her long, scaly neck with his grin growing.

"Just take a little nap!" He flipped Patrasche's entire body upside down, keeping her mouth on his shoulder as he dropped her on her back.

Effectively, Patrasche was knocked unconscious with spit flowing from her mouth onto the floor.

"Better thank her for fightin' so hard for ya!" The blonde glanced from the scaly girl he'd just abused to smirk at Subaru and Otto.

"Wait... Wait!" Subaru pleaded, but the blonde attacker was already striding towards them with swiftness.

"I'm gonna throw you guys outta this forest half-dead... Just alive enough to swear you won't tell a soul about this place!" The stranger dashed with one step, pulling himself up with a foot on the edge of the carriage as a punch was aimed toward Subaru's cheek.

The "thud" of the majestic Earth Dragon hitting the ground upside down, spit flying from her mouth, echoed through the theater like a gunshot.

In the center of the front row, Kenichi Natsuki didn't move. He didn't shout. He sat perfectly still, his chin resting on his interlaced fingers.
