The dim light of the theater screen caught the lenses of his glasses, turning them into two opaque white voids that completely masked his eyes. The shadow of his brow fell heavily over the rest of his face. It was the silent, terrifying stillness of a father.

A father who's watched best girl just be disrespected.

"That... that brute!" Petra's voice broke the silence, high-pitched and vibrating with pure indignation. She stood up, her small fists shaking at the screen. "Garf-san! You're a bully! To do that to Patrasche-sama when she was only trying to protect Subaru-sama?! You need to apologize!"

"Quiet, little sprout," Priscilla's voice cut through the air, smooth and dangerously amused. She fanned herself slowly, her red eyes dancing with a devious glint. "The beast was a fool to challenge a superior predator."

"It was like watching a bug get swatted," Meili giggled, her feet swaying with dark rhythm. "Poor wittle dwagon... she looked so silly with her tongue hanging out."

"He's certainly got the technique for it," Heinkel snorted, though his eyes were narrowed with a soldier's appraisal. "Kid didn't even brace himself for the bite. That's either insane confidence or he's built like a fortress. Probably both."

"Please, everyone!" Frederica stepped forward, her hands clasped in a calming gesture, though her own eyes flickered with a hint of sisterly concern for how the room was perceiving the blonde on screen.

"My brother... Garfiel... he isn't a monster. He is merely... overzealous in his duties. He would never truly harm a creature as noble as an Earth Dragon unless he felt the Sanctuary was in peril."

"Duties?" Roswaal's voice was a low, melodic drawl. He didn't even look at the screen, instead tracing a pattern on the armrest of his chair. To him, Garfiel wasn't a person, he was a piece on a board.

"He is exactly what he was meant to be. A sharp, albeit loud, instrument to ensure the borders of the Sanctuary remain... properly respected. Whether he is 'kind' is irrelevant as l~ooo~ng as he is effective."

Ram let out a long, weary sigh, her gaze fixed on the screen where the blonde was now charging Subaru. "Master is right about one thing: he is very loud. But Frederica is correct as well. Garfiel is a child playing at being a villain. He knows exactly how much force to use to 'rough them up' without causing permanent damage. He's showing off for an audience that isn't there."

She paused, a small, knowing smirk touching her lips. "He's always been prone to dramatics when he thinks he needs to be the 'strongest' in the room. It's pathetic, really."

"It's more than pathetic, it's disappointing," Rem added, her tone uncharacteristically cold.

She remembered the two times she had accompanied Roswaal to the Sanctuary; she had seen Garfiel's strength, but to see it used against a defenseless Otto and a confused Subaru made her grip her skirt.

"Subaru-kun is a guest. To treat a guest with such violence, even if it is 'scaled back,' is a failure of hospitality. You haven't changed at all, Garfiel-kun."

The man in question, the real Garfiel sitting in the theater, finally couldn't take the mounting criticism anymore. He jumped to his feet, his fangs bared in a grin that was half-pride and half-defensive snarl.

"Oi! Watch yer mouths!" Garfiel barked, gesturing wildly at the screen. "Ya see that? I didn't draw a single drop o' blood! I caught 'er mouth on my shoulder 'cause I knew my skin could take it, and I flipped 'er soft! She's havin' a nice nap on the grass, not headin' to the butcher!"

He turned his gaze toward Petra and the parents, his chest puffed out. "My amazin' self has honor! I ended that fight in three seconds so I didn't have to hurt the girl! I was protectin' my home! Ya think I'm gonna let some random carriage roll in without checkin' 'em?"

"You're still scaring them, you idiot," Otto muttered, though he looked relieved that the "real" Garfiel was at least defending his intentions.

"Scarin' 'em is the point!" Garfiel retorted, though he looked a bit more sheepish as he saw the light still reflecting off Kenichi's glasses. "The 'Great Tiger' doesn't invite people in with a hug and a bowl o' soup! He makes 'em swear to keep their traps shut!"

"YOU KNOW FREDERICA, DON'T YOU?!" Subaru shouted with his eyes closed, and every cell in his body clenched, effectively awaiting his fate to be punched by the blonde, but gambled one last time before it was lights out.

It worked...

Garfiel kept his hand frozen in the air as he looked down. "Why the hell did I just hear that name from your nasty mouth?"

Subaru opened his eyes to glare up at the blonde. "Why do you think? Just think about it!"

"Thought about it," Garfiel replied instantly, readying his fist with an excited smile. "Don't know. I'll think after I bash your skull in!"

"Quit jumping to conclusions without thinking! It's because we know her, too!" Subaru shouts rapidly, trying to appeal to the blonde before any attack happens.

"Huh?" Garfiel lowers his fist and leans down to face Subaru's eyes better. "I'm Garfiel. If you guys know her, you must've heard that name, right?!"

"You're..." Subaru hesitated as the blonde above him glared.

"How the hell did you know I have anything to do with Frederica?!"

Subaru's face dropped into a deadpanned expression that was contemplating life itself. "Are you being serious right now?"

Garfiel's sharp teeth glowed with a shine.

On screen, Garfiel's confused demand brought a wave of deadpanned expressions to the theater.

"Is he being serious right now?" Felt blurted out, her feet kicking the air.

"He's always been... simple-minded," Ram sighed, though her eyes softened as she watched the blonde youth on screen.

"Oi! Shut it!" Garfiel barked, though he avoided looking at Frederica. "I was shocked! You don't just expect some random guy from the city to walk in and know your sister! It's weird! It's suspicious! I was protectin' her honor!"

"By almost punching her friend in the face?" Rem asked, her tone dry but lacking its earlier frost. "You really are a handful, Garfiel-san."

"Whatever!" Garfiel sat back down, huffing. "Just watch the screen. The Cap'n is about to see how amazing my amazing self truly is, even if he is a bit of a smart-ass about the family tree."

The theater shifted into a lighter mood, the humor of Garfiel's density providing a much-needed reprieve.

Yet, as Otto stole another glance at Frederica---and she caught him, this time holding the gaze with a soft, playful twinkle in her eyes.

It was clear that while the screen was showing the start of a friendship between Subaru and Garfiel, a different kind of bond was beginning to form in the rows of the theater itself.

The scene changes to show the carriage moving again with Patrasche and Otto at the helm.

"I just got beaten up for nothing." Alone in the driver's seat, Otto was muttering to himself with a frown of contemplation. "What am I supposed to do with this anger?"

Garfiel's voice can be heard from within the carriage behind Otto. "You never shut up, do ya, trader? I apologized, didn't I?"

"You call, 'Hey, sorry, numbskull, I kinda jumped the gun,' an apology?!" Otto exclaimed from the outside of the carriage with fury.

In the theater, the real Otto buried his face in his hands as a ripple of laughter spread through the rows.

"My, Otto-kun," Anastasia chuckled, her eyes gleaming with mischief. "Ya sure do have a lot of bark for someone who was just shakin' like a leaf a minute ago. Is that how ya negotiate with your business partners? Yellin' until your face turns the color of a ripe tomato?"

"It wasn't like that!" Otto protested, though he didn't uncover his face. "Anyone would be angry after being flipped over by a demi-human who hasn't learned the meaning of the word 'wait'!"

Meili giggled from her seat, looking at Otto like he's a fun toy.

"A merchant's only value is his tongue," Ram remarked, her tone carrying that familiar, sharp edge that always managed to sound like a refined insult. "And yet, Otto-kun, yours seems to have tangled itself into a knot of useless, high-pitched rage. It's a miracle the carriage hasn't veered off the road from the sheer weight of your wounded ego."

"You guys are all being incredibly unfair," Otto groaned, slumping back.

Beside him, Garfiel let out a sharp snort. He looked at the screen, then at Frederica, before finally casting a sideways glance at Otto. The defensive snarl he'd been wearing softened into something closer to a begrudging respect.

"Fine, fine," Garfiel muttered, his voice uncharacteristically low. "The 'numbskull' bit was probably uncalled for. I guess... thanks for not let'n my amazing self actually kill the Cap'n before we even got started."

Otto blinked, the fire in his eyes dying down as he looked at the blonde youth. He sighed, a long, weary sound that spoke of a man used to being the universe's punching bag. "I suppose I can't stay angry forever if you're actually going to look like a wet cat. Apology accepted, Garf-san. Just... try to use your words before your fists next time?"

"Don't push your luck, Otto-bro," Garfiel grinned, flashing his fangs, but the malice was gone.

Inside the cart, Subaru was sitting on the floor with his head lowered towards Emilia's sleeping form.

Ignoring Otto's ramblings from the outside, Garfiel is revealed, leisurely sitting back with his arms behind his head.

"Anyway, if you'd shown me that thing sooner, that woulda done it." Subaru turned to the blonde as Garfiel smirks easily. "Gotta act on a ganglion before ya think about it."

"Gangli... huh?" Subaru looked confused, but the blonde continued.

"Lookin' at that hair, I'm guessin' that's the famous Emilia-sama? The silver-haired half-devil that Roswaal's helpin' out."

Subaru stands up with his fists clenched immediately, while Garfiel smirks with satisfaction. "She's a half-elf! Don't you ever call her that again!"

The greatest ragebaiter this side of the Sanctuary hood bellows a laugh. "Hey, look at that! Guess you can put some energy into your voice after all!" He put his hands together and began cracking his knuckles as a twisted expression of excitement overtook his relaxed one. "Wanna know how it feels to be a bazomazo bein' flung back an' forth?"

But all Subaru could respond to this challenge with was bewilderment. "Uh, is there some sort of translation glitch happening between you and me?"

"Huh?"

The theater itself went cold. The term 'half-devil' echoed through the room like an accusation.

In the theater, the real Garfiel looked as though he wanted to physically vanish. The crimson hue of his face deepened, spreading to the tips of his ears.

He stared at his boots, the usual "amazin' self" nowhere to be found.

"I... the amazin' self really was a stupid brat," Garfiel muttered, his voice uncharacteristically low and thick with shame. "Emilia-sama... I shouldn't have said that. I was just tryin' to act tough and pick a fight. I'm sorry."

The sincerity in his voice caused a few heads to turn in surprise.

Kenichi's protective posture softened, his jaw unclamping as he realized the boy wasn't a monster but a child who had been defensive and frightened.

Naoko, sensing the genuine weight of the boy's guilt, leaned over toward his seat between her and Otto. Her eyes were filled with the same warmth she gave Subaru after he'd made a mistake.

"It's alright, Garfiel-kun," she said softly, reaching out to give his shoulder a comforting squeeze. "Sometimes, when we're scared, we use mean words as a shield. You were just protecting your home, weren't you? It takes a big person to admit they were wrong."

Garfiel blinked at her, momentarily stunned by the lack of the scolding he expected. He looked like a cub that had accidentally bitten a hand trying to feed it.

"Hehe... 'half-devil,' was it?"

The voice was light, musical, and entirely unexpected.

Emilia didn't look hurt. Instead, she was looking at Garfiel with a playful, mischievous glint in her eyes that made even Beatrice look up in shock.

She didn't let go of Subaru's hand---she pulled it slightly closer, resting her other hand over his as she addressed the blonde demi-human.

"My, Garfiel," Emilia teased, her tone ringing with a newfound confidence and assertiveness that left several people, including Anastasia, gaping. "I didn't know you thought I was so 'famous'. Though, if I'm a half-devil, does that make you a quarter-monster? Or perhaps just a very grumpy little kitten?"

"H-Huh?!" Garfiel stammered, his eyes widening.

"And all that talk about 'ganglions' and 'bazomazos'..." Emilia continued, her grin widening as she leaned forward. "Don't you know that no one uses such a weird way of speaking? Manners should be reeeeally well respected by someone who cares about the knightly code you claim to respect."

"I... mine amazin' selfs... I was just..." Garfiel was stumbling over his words, his face now a shade of red that rivaled a ripe fruit.

"If you want a real apology accepted," Emilia chirped, her assertiveness clearly catching him off balance, "you have to say it properly. No weird metaphors about ganglions. Just... 'Emilia-sama, I'm sorry for being a big, silly meany-pants.'"

A ripple of genuine laughter spread through the theater. Ricardo let out a booming cackle, while even Julius hid a smile behind his hand.

"I... Fine!" Garfiel huffed, throwing his hands up in defeat, though the defensive snarl was completely gone. "Emilia-sama... I'm sorry for callin' ya that. And... and for the 'bazomazo' stuff. I'll try to use my words better from now on."

"Much better," Emilia said, settling back into her seat with a satisfied nod, her hand still firmly interlaced with Subaru's. "We'll work on your vocabulary later. For now, let's see what other silly things you have to say."

Garfiel couldn't believe the fall from grace he'd been subject to as Emilia of all people criticized his way of speaking. The days of "who says that in this day and age" were now in the past as Subaru was not here to rule the Emilia camp's bullying roster.

"Cap'n's made a monster..." Garfiel muttered, face red.

But before either could continue, Emilia began to stir from Subaru's side.

"Emilia!" Subaru moved to kneel in front of her, waiting for her to rise in her seat. He had a bright smile on his face while Garfiel stared at them with a calm face. "I'm so glad you're okay!"

"Subaru?" Emilia looked at him and then gasped when she noticed Garfiel in the carriage with them. She immediately moved forward, standing over the kneeling Subaru and stretching her arms out to cover him, looking at the blonde with resolve and protectiveness.

"Who are you?! Just so you know, I won't let you lay a finger on Subaru!"

"Hang on, Emilia-tan!" Subaru stands up and holds his hands out to Emilia with a sheepish expression. "I appreciate it, but as a guy, I have mixed feelings about this! Really, I'm fine!"

The laughter that this obtained could be a death sentence for any person's reputation in the hood. Especially since it's Kenichi leading the charge while Otto took the chance to enact the code of his brotherhood with Subaru and laugh at his emasculation.

Everyone else, like Julius, Heinkel, and Felix, were just being petty. While Hetaro just wanted to be involved and Schult wasn't allowed to laugh by a glare from Priscilla and a small pat from Meili who was bored to death.

Emilia, sitting in her theater seat, looked between the laughing men and the screen with a look of pure, unadulterated bafflement. She didn't understand the joke at all. Her hands were still tightly gripping Subaru's sleeve, her brow furrowed in a cute, confused pout.

"But... why are they laughing?" Emilia asked, her voice small and genuinely perplexed. "Garfiel looked dangerous! And Subaru was still hurt from everything before. Why wouldn't I protect him? Is it... is it wrong to want to keep him safe?"

"It's not wrong, Emilia-sama," Rem's voice cut through the laughter, but it wasn't a comfort. The maid stood up slightly in her seat, her blue eyes flashing with a competitive fire as she looked at the screen and then back at the half-elf. "But you shouldn't feel like you have to do it alone."

Rem turned her gaze toward the back of the room, her voice gaining a sharp, formal edge. "For the record, I would also protect Subaru-kun with my life. Whether he has 'mixed feelings' or not, I will be the shield that stands before him!"

The theater exploded into a fresh wave of noise.

Felix was practically rolling in his seat, his ears twitching with every laugh. "He's so worried about being the knight, but he's clearly the princess of the camp! Subaru-kuyn needs all her guardians to keep the mean monsters away!"

Amidst the chaos of the men's laughter and the girls' protective declarations, a small, bright voice rang out from the row behind them.

"I think it's wonderful!"

Petra was leaning forward, her face glowing with delight. "Emilia-sama looks so cool! To stand up like that... it proves how much she cares! Subaru-sama is lucky to have someone who doesn't care about 'manly feelings' when his safety is on the line!"

Emilia blinked, her confused expression softening as she looked back at the little maid. "You... you think so, Petra?"

"I know so!" Petra nodded vigorously.

Naoko reached over and patted Emilia's hand, her giggles finally subsiding into a gentle, knowing smile. "Don't mind them, Emilia-chan. Men are very silly about their pride. But as a mother, seeing you stand up for my son like that... it makes me very, very happy. You're exactly the kind of girl I hoped he'd find."

Emilia's face turned a shade of pink that rivaled the dawn, her silver hair falling forward as she looked down at Subaru's hand.

"I'm not gonna do anything," Garfiel says while leaning his head back into his hand and grinning with closed eyes. "I don't want Ram givin' me hell later."

"Ram?" Emilia blinked in surprise, lowering her protective arms while Subaru gave her a smile of assurance.

"This is Garfiel."

"Garfiel... The one Frederica told us about?" Emilia looked down at the relaxed blonde boy with curiosity.

"Yeah, and he's showing us the way to the Sanctuary now." Subaru glares down at Garfiel, taking a more stern tone. "Ram and the villagers from Arlam are there... right?"

"Don't wanna believe me? That's your call." Garfiel opened his eyes to give them a sinister grin. "Ain't no turnin' back now, though. We are already past the barrier."

Emilia's eyes widened in recognition. "Then we're in the Sanctuary right now?"

"C'mon, gimme a break," Garfiel calls out, making them stop looking around uselessly. "Weren't you asleep 'cause you got close to the barrier?"

"The barrier?" Emilia blinked. "Come to think of it, when the stone glowed... It was at the same moment."

Subaru held the blue crystal given to them by Frederica in his palm and looked at it.

"So it was reacting to the barrier, then?" Subaru asked Garfiel, but the blonde only glared at the crystal in Subaru's hand. "But why did only Emilia-tan pass out? I mean, I got transported somewhere, but Otto---"

A loud scoff cut Subaru off and gained Emilia's attention.

"You know damn well why the barrier reacted to her and not you two." He held an ugly grin on his face that affected Emilia.

"It's not just half-elves that thing reacts to. It works on me and a lot of others, too. That's 'cause..."

When Garfiel trailed off, Emilia continued for him.

"Because you have mixed blood, too?"

Garfiel only replied with a snort.

"So the Sanctuary is a place where demihumans with mixed blood live?" Subaru asked as Garfiel stood up from his perch, Otto looking in from the driver's window with a curious expression.

"We welcome you, Emilia-sama, and your two escorts, too!" He made a show for everyone to look ahead, with Otto grunting in surprise since he was outside in the driver's area and could see first before anyone.

Subaru and Emilia leaned out of the windows of the moving cart.

"Roswaal likes to go around callin' it the Sanctuary, but it ain't the kinda place that deserves such a nice name." Garfiel's narration of the town was sinister and hopeless as Subaru observed ruined buildings with moss and fungi overtake roads and fences alike. "Nothin' there but a hodgepodge of halfwits."

A lot of people gathered around to look at the cart as Otto steered Patrasche. All of them with animal hearts and tails, some even having horns on their heads.

"It's just a dead-end testing ground."

People looked afraid as the carriage continued to roll through the town to the point that Otto seemed uncomfortable. He quickly began looking around. "Where do I..."

Suddenly, a stocking-covered foot walked its way infront of Patrasche, with the owner of said foot speaking immediately.

"You're back. It took you long enough."

Subaru opened the driver's window immediately, gazing at the owner of the voice with a wide smile.

"Ram!"

"Sanctuary..." Crusch repeated the word, her voice dripping with irony. She leaned forward, her eyes narrowing as the camera panned over a crumbling stone wall covered in thick, sickly moss. "I have seen refugee camps on the borders of the Empire that looked more hospitable than this. Roswaal-sama, your definition of 'Sanctuary' is... illuminating."

Roswaal didn't answer. He sat perfectly still, his mismatched eyes fixed on the ruins of the town he technically owned.

"Look at those houses," Naoko whispered, her hand flying to her mouth. "Kenichi, the roofs are falling in. There's... is that mold growing on the window frames?"

Kenichi's jaw was set tight. To him, this looked like the aftermath of a natural disaster that everyone had simply given up on fixing. "It's a slum." He looked at the screen, then at the sleeping Subaru. "Our son spent weeks here? In this squalor?"

"It's not just the buildings," Ricardo rumbled from the back. The large wolf-man's voice was uncharacteristically low, a dangerous growl vibrating in his chest. "Look at the folks. Look at their eyes."

Mimi and Hetaro, usually so full of energy, were huddled close to Ricardo's sides. They watched as the screen showed a group of demihumans---children with floppy ears and scales, adults with horns---watching the carriage with hollow, fearful expressions.

"They look... hungry," Mimi whispered, her usual cheer replaced by a hauntingly quiet observation. "And sad. Why are they so sad?"

"Because they're 'half-bloods', Mimi," Tivey answered, his monocle reflecting the gray light of the screen. "In the world outside, they're shunned. And here, in the place that's supposed to protect them... they're living in a graveyard. It's a ghetto, that's what it is."

Ricardo's fist slammed into his palm. "I've seen some bad shit in the mercenary life, but callin' a dump like this a 'Sanctuary' is a spit in the face to every demihuman tryin' to make a life. If this is how the Mathers family 'protects' its people, then the Mathers family is failin'."

"A testing ground," Julius noted, his pen scratching furiously against his paper. "He called it a 'dead-end testing ground.' Not a town. Not a refuge. A laboratory." He looked at Anastasia. "My lady, this is a violation of the Kingdom's charters on the treatment of demi-human populations. If this were public knowledge..."

"If it were public knowledge, the Council would've seized it years ago," Anastasia finished for him, her greed replaced by a sharp, political disgust. "But it's a 'Witch's Tomb.' That's the shield Roswaal uses to keep prying eyes out. He's hidin' a tragedy behind a legend."

"Don't listen to that blonde brat, in fact!" Beatrice snapped, though her own eyes were fixed on the ruins with a hint of sorrow. "Mother... Mother designed this place as a laboratory for the soul, I suppose. It was never meant to be a town. That the people here have suffered is a result of time and neglect, not just the barrier's nature!"

But the theater-goers couldn't shake the image of the ruined buildings and the fearful eyes of the residents.

For the parents, it was a terrifying look at the world their son was forced to survive in.

For the demihumans, it was a reminder of the cruelty of bloodlines.

And for the knights, it was the first piece of evidence that the "Sanctuary" was a lie built on the bones of a Witch.

"Well," Anastasia leaned back, her eyes cold. "The 'Warden' wanted us to see the truth. I think we're startin' to see the rot."

The screen changes to show the maid standing tall with her arms crossed.

"I haven't a clue which Barusu you are, but I'm disappointed by how long it took you to get here. Ah, but I suppose I was a fool to expect more of you." Ram put her hands on her hips while glaring at him.

Subaru had to show offense at this as he clenched his fists. "If your story is that 'haven't a clue' who I am, stick to it!"

"Hmph." Ram huffed with a simple wave of her hand, as if to brush him off.

"Kind of nice to see someone treating him like he needs to be treated." Kenichi grinned at the Ram on screen.

"Ram has her own charm when she's interacting with Subaru-kun." Rem whispered to the parents with a proud smile on her face.

"I have a feeling that my sister is saying something really incorrect." Ram sighed in the back, not being able to hear the parents and her sister converse about her. Though her eyes turned back up to look at Subaru with a slight smile.

Once everyone's out of the carriage, Ram straightens her posture with her hands moving infront of her. "Emilia-sama, I welcome you. Roswaal-sama is waiting inside. Allow me to show you the way."

Ram turned around but paused to look behind Emilia. "Garf, take the ground dragon and carriage somewhere suitable."

"Is that any way to treat me?" Garfiel looked bothered by the disrespect in her tone. But it's quickly replaced with a smile. "That's just how I like it, though."

"Ew... That's gross." Petra commented with her tongue out.

"I can treat ya like that!" Mimi announces with a giggle while her brothers just sputter and stutter apologies as Garfiel gets a red blush on his face.

Ram had the grace not to comment and sighed at everyone as if they were children. Which they were, for the record.

He turned to Otto and waved at him. "Yo, cabbie, follow me."

"Are you referring to me?! That's the worst name you've called me yet!" Otto's outrage could burn worlds as he is subject to more bullying. "Also, I have to be alone with you?!"

With Emilia and Ram watching, Subaru gave Otto a thumbs up and a supportive grin.

"I'll pick up what's left of you." He told Otto with his teeth shining like the hero best friend he was destined to be.

"WHY AM I BEING TREATED SO ROUGHLY?!" Otto whined, but Subaru only chuckled at him.

"What are you talking about? When I was unconscious, Emilia only stayed safe because you were there." Subaru looked up at Otto and gave him a grin. "I'm grateful."

"O-Oh?" The merchant clearly was awestruck at receiving such common decency. "W-Well, as long as you're appreciative... Right." With that, Otto drove the carriage away.

Subaru continued to look on with his lips forming a grin. "He's too easy."

"NATSUKI-SAAAAAAN!"

Otto's scream of pure, unadulterated betrayal ripped through the theater, nearly drowning out the explosive laughter that followed. The merchant was standing up, pointing a trembling finger at the screen as if he could strike the animated Subaru down with his sheer indignation.

"You monster! You absolute demon!" Otto wailed, his face buried in his hands as he sank back into his seat. "I was so moved! I felt so appreciated! I was ready to follow you into the fire because of that smile, and you were just... you were just playing me like a cheap lute!"

"Pwahahahaha!" Ricardo's laughter was like a physical blow, shaking the seats of everyone in the row. "Oh, kid, you really are a soft touch! 'He's too easy!' I'm gonna be usin' that one for a week!"

"It's true, though!" Felt barked, her feet kicking the air in delight. "Big Bro's got you on a leash, Cabbie! One nice word and you're waggin' your tail!"

Even Kenichi and Naoko couldn't hold back. Wiping tears from his eyes, he leaned against his wife.

"He really does take after me, doesn't he? That 'Hero Smile' is a lethal weapon in the Natsuki household. Sorry, Otto-kun, you never stood a chance."

Mimi and Hetaro were giggling uncontrollably, with Mimi even imitating Subaru's thumbs-up toward a miserable-looking Otto.

However, amidst the cacophony of mirth, one corner of the theater remained a tundra of silence.

Julius sat rigid, his hands clasped so tightly over his notebook that the paper was beginning to crinkle. He wasn't laughing. In fact, his eyes were fixed on Otto and Garfiel with a gaze that was cold, sharp, and deeply wounded.

He remembered the earlier exchange---the way Garfiel had looked him in the eye and told him to "fuck off" with a feral intensity that left no room for negotiation. He remembered Otto's desperate, frantic wave to stop any violence, not out of a desire for peace, but out of a need to protect the secrets of the Emilia camp.

Allies, Julius thought, the word tasting like ash in his mouth.

"Is something wrong, Julius-san?" Tivey asked softly, noticing the knight's pallor.

"Nothing," Julius replied, his voice a clipped, metallic rasp. He didn't look at the small merchant. He didn't look at the screen. He kept his eyes on the back of Otto's head. "I am merely realizing that the 'wall' around the Sanctuary isn't just made of stone and magic. It is made of people who would rather let their 'allies' stumble in the dark than offer a single hand of truth."

He looks back at the girls behind him, his eyes particularly showing a serious focus on Ram.

The maid noticed immediately, putting her hands on her hips. "What is it?"

Subaru's expression is replaced by an unsure smile. "Oh, just... Wondering how you were doing." He glanced down with a small smile that didn't reach his eyes. "You remember what was going on the last time I saw you."

Flashbacks to Ram and Wilhelm informing Emilia to evacuate the mansion before the cult's incursion on Roswaal's lands played, with Subaru standing behind them in Emilia's appearance-concealing hoodie.

"I couldn't get in touch with you after that, and to be honest, I was really worried."

"Being worried about by Pal... Must be an experience, huh?" Al, for the first time since a long while in the theater, had decided to remind Ram of their friendship by checking in on her. "Are you really... just an older sister in disguise?"

Ram scoffed, leaning away from Roswaal to wink at the helmet wearing man in the same row as them. "Why must you bring such a horrifying link between me and someone like Barusu? Absolutely vile."

Ram held him with a small smile, putting a hand to her chest. "As you can see, I am as lovely and healthy as ever." Ram's smile turned kind and informed him with warmth about more. "The villagers of Arlam are all safe, too."

Subaru's face looked like a thousand needles had suddenly disappeared from beneath his feet at the news.

"You can be at ease." Ram encouraged him softly as relief flooded through him.

"She was really helping him relax? Subaru really found such amazing people for his company." Naoko commented with her face beaming. "I can't imagine to understand how he feels with such good friends. I'm so happy, Kenichi... I'm so glad!"

"Honey... You look like you're about to cry!" Kenichi held on to his wife with a hint of worry and amusement.

Rem smiled slightly at the parents with a sad tint to it.

"I see." Subaru smiled widely... But the light didn't reach his eyes when he suddenly looked down. "Yeah, that's... a real relief."

A flashback of Ram and Rem teasing Subaru played in his mind.

"That's a relief." He repeated, hollow, empty, and without looking Ram in the eyes.

Ram seemed to notice that something was off and kept her gaze on him with an inquiring eyebrow raise.

Subaru stuttered for a moment before looking away from her again. "U-Um, listen..." he couldn't continue as he bit his lip, holding back a lot of anguish that once it began, couldn't be stopped.

"..." Rem looked at the floor, unable to help with any of Subaru's pain as his struggle was shown to the entire theater.

"Man... What a doomed atmosphere we got right now..." Ricardo commented with guilt as the beastman's ears drooped. His lofty grin seemed to shrink into nothing.

"Oh..." Naoko's earlier excitement died as she remembered what had been long forgotten for the people in the screen's world.

"Rem... He's the only one who remembers her and..." Emilia struggled to continue her sentence.

"It's not like you have to say it out loud, in fact." Beatrice sighed, her face dropping as well. "It's clear for all to see, I suppose."

"At least he'd done the job and told you in the end... He cared." Roswaal commented with a bit of comfort, trying to do his part for Ram, seeing that no one was attending to her.

But the pink-haired maid leaned away from his touch, crossing her arms. "Barusu wouldn't have ever failed to do his duty... He didn't have a book to anchor him."

So... Emilia decided to call from his side. "Hey, Ram... This really isn't a place to stand around and talk. Would you show us inside?"

"Certainly." Ram gave a polite bow to Emilia and turned toward the house behind her, leading them both.

As Ram opened the door and entered, Emilia stepped towards Subaru with a look of concern. "Subaru, are you all right?"

"Yeah." He grumbled with a look of distance at his shoes.

"I'm sorry for interrupting you, but..." Emilia gave him a guilty look, unable to find the words.

Subaru shook his head. "No, I get it. I just freaked out a little. For her to not even ask about Rem when all this is going on..." Subaru smiled with the most fake, stretched smile he could muster. "I'm pathetic, huh?"

"Why does he always say that?" Emilia muttered with a tired sigh, bringing her hand closer to his again so she can squeeze at it tightly. "Can't he see how much it hurts to hear something like that from him?"

Most of the theater had gone quiet and uncomfortable by the notion of Subaru's self esteem. His parents in particular found what they were hearing appalling.

"He's apologizing for something that's not even his fault... I saw what he did for the girl when she was found... We all did..." Naoko couldn't but close her eyes. "This son of mine... He's so small in his mind."

"Only in his mind, honey." Kenichi kissed his wife on her head, not wishing her to feel alone in her love for their son.

Emilia's eyes hardened, and she moved immediately to clench her hands around his, stepping closer to look him in the eye. "It's only natural to be scared. I'll never think that's pathetic."

"Let's go," she said, giving him a smile of encouragement. "We'll fix what happened to Rem and everything else, too."

"Got it." Subaru's eyes closed with his tension disappearing from his shoulders as Emilia held his hand. "No matter what happens, I'll be your meat shield and keep you safe."

"What a different context that gives..." Felix didn't find the humor but he had to give a fake chuckle anyway, tail switching chairs out of discomfort. "... Forget I said that."

The healer noticed how everyone seemed uncomfortable by the idea, especially the parents, who couldn't stomach the thought of how literal that 'meat shield' comment might have been.

The scene changes to the household in the Sanctuary, now encased in the afterglow of the sunset as nighttime began.

"Ah, Emilia-sama and Subaru-kun. It feels like it's been so very long since I last saw y~ooo~u." Roswaal's voice filled the screen as the scene changed to show Subaru and Emilia standing in a room with shocked faces.

"I was going to slug you when I saw you again, but now..." Subaru mumbles while staring very openly toward Roswaal's form. (Pause) "What the heck happened?"

Before Subaru and Emilia, the blue-haired mage was looking at them with only his yellow eye open, bringing no shame to the rest of what his body was revealing.

The man who sat before them was covered from the neck to the very end of the torso with bandaging and wound covering treatments. A white bandage was wrapped around his head, also.

"What could have hurt you so badly?" Emilia asked as Roswaal leaned back on the bed where his body was lying.

"Good grief..." Kenichi whispered, his hands tightening on the armrests. "He looks like he went through a meat grinder. I thought you said this guy was a big-shot lord? A powerful wizard?"

"He is," Wilhelm answered, his eyes narrowing as he leaned forward. "Which is precisely why this sight is so disturbing. To see a mage of his rank incapacitated to this degree... it implies an opponent or a trial of overwhelming power."

"Ferris, what do you see?" Crusch asked, her voice low.

Felix didn't answer immediately. He was standing, his cat-like eyes scanning every inch of the screen with clinical precision. "Those aren't just cuts or bruises, Crusch-sama. Look at the way the bandages are wrapped around his torso... they're thickest over his mana gate. And the burns on his face---those aren't from a fire. That's magical backlash. It's as if his own power turned inside out and tried to consume him, nya."

"I do w~ooo~nder why you would be asking your knight about the extent of my injuries when I'm sitting right h~eee~re with you all, hm?" Roswaal hummed in his seat. "I don't appreciate being ignored."

"You gotta start learning how to be ignored then," Al snorted. "Because no one in this room is trying to get answers from you."

"That is a little hurtful," Roswaal shook his head, though he kept his eccentric smile. "Finally something of interest."

"Well, now, where shall I beg~iii~n?" Roswaal smiled as he was about to begin his yapping session before Subaru cut him off with all the disrespect a Crip can give to a Blood like the Margrave.

"Hey, before that, just one thing... The alliance with Crusch-san is on. Now, are you satisfied with the results you got from abandoning me?" Subaru's glare foretold a lot.

Roswaal grinned without hiding his satisfaction. "Yes, most satisf~iii~ed. Truly, truly, you were the once-in-a-lifetime windfall I had long waited for."

Many suspicious glances were given toward Roswaal from the entire Emilia camp who kept to themselves and didn't share anything with anyone, and the Anastasia camp who'd been planning on using every detail from what they're learning as possible.

"Subaru was abandoned by this guy?" Kenichi noted beside his wife, looking at the screen with a slight frown.

Julius tensed at the memory of what happened in the capital and kept his focus on his notes to hide himself from the judgment of the parents should that scene play before them.

Subaru sighed as Emilia looked at him with increasing agitation. "Oh, really?" He looked down, as if dealing with something inside his head.

Seeing him quiet, Emilia decided to continue for Subaru. "Roswaal," she called softly from Subaru's side. "What is this place?"

"Since we got here... no, since we made contact with the barrier... I've had this anxious feeling in my chest, and I can't relax." The screen showed landscape shots of the deycribid villages of the Sanctuary.

"It's the mana density, I suppose," Beatrice noted, her drills unusually still. "A place where a Witch's soul is anchored will always reject those who do not belong. It is a house that has forgotten how to be a home, in fact."

"I've heard it called the 'Sanctuary,' but it doesn't seem like one to me at all." Emilia's voice softens along with her eyes as she looks to the floor. "In fact, it feels more like..."

When she herself grew quieter than Subaru, there was no one in the room but Roswaal to finish her sentence, with his face adopting a most sinister expression on his face with both his multi-colored eyes shining a glint.

"The Witch's Graveyard?" Roswaal's voice was filled with warmth, which increased the disturbing, obsessive nature of how he treated this reveal. So much so that Emilia stepped back a bit.

"Does it seem to make far more sense to call it th~aaa~t?"

"What do you mean?" Emilia held her hands to her chest with a confused yet worried frown.

"Exactly what I said, nothing more or less." Roswaal looked down at his hand as his tone became more rueful. "This is where the Witch of Greed, Echidna, met her end long ago, and in my mind, it is worth calling a 'Sanctuary.'"

"Look at his face," Kenichi said, his voice hard. "He's not talking about a cemetery. He's talking about a holy land. That's the look of a man who's found his religion, and he's willing to burn everything else to keep it."

"He's terrifying," Naoko added, her grip on Subaru's hand tightening. "How can he call a graveyard a 'Sanctuary' with such a happy voice? It's not right. Subaru... please don't listen to him."

"I do agree that this tone from the Margrave is more than... worshipful." Crusch narrowed her eyes as her and Anastasia scrutinized the man far more than anyone else in the room. While Felt and Priscilla ignored Roswaal's existence as someone of worthless existence.

"Echidna..." Subaru's face was struck with a weird form of shock, staring off into space.

"Subaru? Are you all right?" Emilia asks him from the side.

Subaru swallowed and shook his head, waving his hand with an easy smile. "Yeah, I'm fine."

"He's choking on it," Al noted, his helmet tilting as he watched Subaru struggle. "Can't say anything, Pal."

"So he was holding on to that meeting while in that moment with us?" Emilia looked sympathetic toward Subaru, her eyes now far more knowing.

"This will make him more isolated than ever," Julius sighed, showing the same worry Reinhard was feeling while watching this.

He turns to Roswaal with a frown. "And why are you overseeing a place with that kind of backstory?"

"It's quite simple," Roswaal answered with an easy smile. "This land has been overseen by the heads of the Mathers family for generations."

Julius's pen snapped in his hand. The sharp crack echoed in the relatively quiet theater.

"Generations," the Finest of Knights whispered, his golden eyes narrowing into dangerous slits as he stared at the back of the theater. "The Mathers domain has harbored the soul of a Witch for generations... and the Council was entirely blind to it."

"Or they chose to look the other way, nya," Felix added, his own ears pinned back in clear distaste. "Either way, it stinks."

"Then the Mathers family and the Witch of Greed have---" Emilia's conclusion was cut short by a firm, almost a burst, of emotion from the eccentric man standing before them.

"Echidna." He corrected.

The sudden sharpness in his voice on the screen made several people in the audience flinch. It wasn't just the interruption; it was the raw, uncharacteristic reverence laced within that single word. It lacked his usual sing-song drawl, replacing it with a deadly serious devotion.

"Huh?" Emilia squeaked softly.

Roswaal's smile was easy and warm, unusually so, as he waved a bandaged arm forward in a welcoming manner.

"He... he interrupted Emilia-sama?" Rem blinked, her protective instincts flaring. The sheer disrespect shown to a Royal Candidate was jarring enough, but the reason for it was what truly unsettled the maid. "Roswaal-sama... What have you done?"

"Please, when you speak of her, do use her name. The name of 'Witch of Greed' makes her sound so evil." He waved a finger at her. "That's not very nice, is it?"

"A Witch... He speaks of a Witch as if she were a maiden to be courted," Crusch said, her voice laced with absolute disgust. Even without actively focusing, the wind reading around the Margrave in the back row must have been a hurricane of twisted, sickening devotion.

In that back row, the real Roswaal didn't even attempt to defend his on-screen counterpart. He simply closed his mismatched eyes, a serene, genuinely fond smile gracing his painted lips.

"She is no mere Witch to me," Roswaal whispered into the dark, a statement meant for no one but himself, though Ram heard it clearly. The pink-haired maid simply tightened her grip on her own skirt, her eyes downcast in quiet resignation.

"You really are a pathetic simp, Margrave."

The voice was dry, metallic, and devoid of any amusement. Everyone turned to look at Al. The helmeted knight wasn't looking at Roswaal; his gaze was fixed squarely on the screen, his one hand clenched into a trembling fist upon his knee. "Defending her name like she gives a damn about yours... Teacher really did a number on your head, didn't she?"

Priscilla didn't reprimand her knight. She merely observed the unfolding drama from behind her fan, her red eyes gleaming with cruel entertainment. "A fool dancing on the strings of a dead woman. How delightfully tragic."

Kenichi swallowed hard, pulling Naoko a little closer. The warm, fatherly demeanor he usually carried was replaced by a cold, protective instinct. "Honey... I don't think that clown guy is just a weird boss anymore."

"He looks..." Naoko shivered, rubbing her arms as she stared at the screen. "He looks like one of those fanatics on the news back home. The ones who smile right before they do something terrible for their cult."

Kenichi nodded grimly. "Yeah. He's got that exact look."

"Um, then... the Mathers family and Echidna have been acquainted for a long time, and that's why they've always overseen this place?" Emilia asked with her hand lowering from her chest, but the other staying on Puck's jewel.

"Acquainted is putting it far too lightly, I suppose," Beatrice murmured from Subaru's lap, her voice barely above a whisper. Her butterfly-patterned eyes looked at Roswaal with a mixture of pity and deep-seated disdain. "To be so thoroughly shackled by a past that Mother herself would have already discarded... You truly are a pitiful man, Roswaal."

"If this is true," Reinhard finally spoke up, his voice steady but carrying the immense weight of the Sword Saint's authority, "then Margrave Mathers, your actions border on high treason against the Kingdom of Lugunica."

Julius nodded firmly in agreement, standing from his seat to look directly at Roswaal. "I must agree. Hiding a Witch's grave, actively revering her name, and admonishing a Royal Candidate for speaking ill of a historical calamity... This cannot go unanswered once we return to the capital."

Roswaal finally opened his eyes, glancing over his shoulder at the assembled knights with an infuriatingly calm expression. "Treason, you saaaaay? I merely maintain the lands passed down to me. What lies beneath them is simply... family history."

"Family history my ass," Felt barked from the front, throwing her hands up in the air. "You're a cultist in a clown suit! Big Bro's out here dying on repeat, and he's working for a total lunatic!"

"Yes, though to s~aaa~y we 'oversee' it is a bit of an exaggeration. Echidna's barrier prevents entry by outsiders who fail to follow the forest's proper procedure. In addition, the barrier has certain effects on those whose blood meets certain conditions."

Emilia flinched in her seat, her hand instinctively rising to her forehead as the phantom pain of the barrier rejecting her half-elf blood resurfaced. Next to her, Naoko noticed the gesture and gently placed a comforting hand on the girl's shoulder, her motherly instincts flaring up.

"He knew," Crusch stated, her voice slicing through the silence like a drawn blade. Her amber eyes narrowed dangerously as she turned her head to look at the back row. "You sent her in there fully aware of what the barrier would do to her."

Roswaal didn't offer a defense, his mismatched eyes remaining fixed on the screen with an infuriatingly calm disposition.

A sinister bell was playing faintly in the background as Roswaal's eyes turned to her.

"Did you not experience this yourself, Emilia-sama?"

"You bastard," Garfiel snarled from his seat, his fangs bared. The armrests of his chair groaned under the sudden, crushing grip of his hands. "Playin' games with 'em while they don't know shit about what yer really up to."

"Garf-san, please," Otto whispered frantically, though he didn't look entirely opposed to the blonde's anger. The merchant's face was pale, realizing just how manipulative their employer was truly being at this exact moment in time.

Emilia looked uncomfortable before Subaru interjected.

"And how did you end up in such a sorry state?"

"Yeah, who kicked yer ass?" Felt barked a short, humorless laugh. "Because whoever it was, I'd like to shake their hand. They didn't hit ya nearly hard enough, in my opinion."

"Such vulgarity, yet I find myself inclined to agree with the sentiment," Priscilla hid a cruel smirk behind her fan, her red eyes gleaming with amusement at the Margrave's battered state on screen.

Roswaal held a small smile and allowed Ram, who was standing beside him, to speak to that.

"Did you not find it at all strange, Emilia-sama?" Ram spoke to the half-elf.

Anastasia sighed, rubbing the bridge of her nose beneath her scarf. "Spirits above... they really are too pure for this world. Ya can practically see the wool being pulled over their eyes."

"It is a painful level of trust," Julius agreed softly, pausing his note-taking to look at the screen with a mixture of pity and frustration. "To not even question why the refugees hadn't returned... Natsuki-san and Emilia-sama rely too heavily on the assumed goodwill of their allies."

"That ain't a flaw, Knight-boy," Al's gruff voice filtered through his helmet. "It's just a damn shame they put that trust in a snake."

"Huh?" She replied once again, clearly more confused.

"That all the people who fled the Sanctuary, as well as Roswaal-sama and myself, stayed here and didn't return to the mansion?"

"Well..." Emilia looked to Subaru as he did to her, both having the same curiosity clear on their faces.

"I mean, you stayed here because you're hurt, right?" Subaru put his hands on his hips, looking at the injured man on the bed.

In the audience, Kenichi let out a long, heavy sigh, letting his head fall back against his seat.

"He's so dense sometimes," Kenichi muttered, a strained, loving exasperation in his voice. "I taught him to look for the good in people, but maybe I should have taught him to look for the trapdoors too."

"He's just trying to be optimistic, dear," Naoko whispered back, though she too looked incredibly anxious at the corner her son was unknowingly walking into.

Roswaal's smile grew even more. "We are all being held under house arrest here in the Sanctu~aaa~ry."

"Huh?!" Emilia and Subaru both let out in unison.

"Myself, Ram, and the people of Arlam... Oh," Roswaal closed his blue eye while keeping the yellow one smiling at them both, "and you two as well, from the moment you entered h~eee~re."

"House arrest?!" Heinkel snorted in the back. "You lured your own damn Royal Candidate into a hostage situation?!"

"This is treason," Reinhard stated, his voice devoid of its usual warmth. The Sword Saint slowly turned his head to face Roswaal, his blue eyes icy and unyielding. "To hold citizens of Arlam and a Royal Candidate against their will, knowing full well they could not escape... Margrave Mathers, your actions border on a declaration of war against the Kingdom's laws."

"Is it treason if it occurs within my own priv~aaa~te domain?" Roswaal finally spoke in the theater, his singsong tone dripping with condescension. "They entered of their own free will, did they not?"

"They entered because you orchestrated it, you clown!" Crusch slammed her fist onto her armrest, her usual composure completely shattering. "You held the villagers hostage to force their hand!"

"I've done it to help release them." Roswaal's voice dropped the accent for one second, which forced Reinhard and Crusch back as the screen above continued to show what was hidden from the rest of them.

Garfiel turned from Otto's curious gaze.

"So that's it." Kenichi's voice wasn't loud, but it cut through the shouting of the knights and candidates. The father sat rigid, his hands balled into tight fists on his lap. He looked at the screen, at the smiling, bruised face of the man who had just trapped his son.

"Kenichi..." Naoko whimpered, holding his arm tightly.

"He didn't just wander into a bad situation," Kenichi continued, his voice trembling with a terrifying, suppressed paternal rage. He slowly turned in his seat, locking eyes with the Margrave. There was no innocence or playful fatherly demeanor left in him. "You put a leash on my boy. You dragged him into this cage."

"A necessary piece for the board, Natsuki-san," Roswaal replied smoothly, though Ram subtly shifted her stance, placing herself marginally more between her master and the furious father.

"I don't care about your board," Kenichi practically growled, leaning forward. "If you think you can just play games with my son's life---"

"He's already played it, Pops," Garfiel interrupted, his voice surprisingly hollow. The blonde teen wasn't yelling anymore; he was just staring at the screen with an empty look. "Cap'n's trapped. Emilia-sama's trapped. And there ain't a damn thing any of 'em can do about it without dyin'."

The harsh reminder of Subaru's deaths in the opening instantly smothered the fiery outrage in the room, replacing it with a suffocating blanket of dread.

Beatrice, who had remained perfectly silent throughout the entire exchange, simply tightened her tiny grip on Subaru's jacket, burying her face into his chest as the reality of his impending, inescapable suffering settled over them all.

Suddenly, the door to the room opened as Garfiel barged inside. "The hell? You guys are still flappin' your gums in here?"

In the theater, the audience Garfiel immediately slouched down in his seat, pulling his collar up over his neck. "Aw, man... here it comes," he muttered, his earlier rage at Roswaal momentarily replaced by a deep, burning embarrassment.

Otto couldn't help but give his blonde friend a deadpan stare. "Really, Garfiel? Barging in like a thug? We were just talking about how much of a threat you were acting like..."

"Shaddup, bro! M' just playin' the part!" Garfiel hissed, though his cheeks were tinting pink.

"House arrest... Then are Roswaal's injuries..." Emilia glared as she turned to Garfiel with a hint of accusation in her eyes.

"Nope," Garfiel smirked to himself as he cut her off. "He got hurt 'cause he was rejected by the trail."

"Trail?" Subaru and Emilia looked even more baffled. "What does that mean?"

"Rejected by the trial..." Julius echoed the word from his seat, his pen tapping against his notebook. He turned his sharp gaze toward the back row. "So the Margrave attempted to conquer this graveyard himself and was physically repelled. I assume this was no simple magical feedback, considering the state of your body, Roswaal-sama."

Roswaal simply smiled, his mismatched eyes offering nothing but a hollow, amused glint. "Such is the fate of those who overstep their bounds, Julius-kun. The Sanctuary is quite... particular about its guests."

"Speaking of particularities..." Tivey whispered to Julius. "How come you're not aiming for his head no more?"

Julius eyed Otto's back for a second and then shook his head. "We need all of the information we need."

"You've seen what happens when you come in contact with the barrier, right?" Garfiel gave an evil grin as he explained. "Mixed-bloods who go through that and still come in here are never allowed to leave."

"Does that mean Emilia-tan too?!" Subaru looked at Emilia in shock as she herself let out a small gasp.

"The barrier doesn't mean squat to most people. Doesn't that seem a little unfair, Emilia-sama?" Garfiel crossed his arms and began talking in a normal tone instead of his exaggerated evil one. "The only way to break the barrier is to go through the graveyard's trial, but if anyone who isn't a mixed-blood tries it..."

He paused as Roswaal smiled at him from the side, with Ram watching in silence.

Garfiel's grin widened to show his sharp teeth. "All the villagers are rounded up in one place. So long as they don't act up, we won't hurt 'em. They even get three meals and a nap every day!"

The sheer tension that had been building in the theater popped like a balloon against a needle.

For a second, nobody spoke. They merely processed the terms of this supposedly brutal hostage situation.

Then, a loud, obnoxious snort echoed from the front row.

Felt threw her head back and began cackling, slapping her knee. "Three meals?! And a nap?! Spirits preserve us, what a terrifying villain! They're gonna sleep 'em to death!"

"Hey!" Garfiel shouted from his seat, his face completely flushed. "I was tryin' to be intimidatin'!"

"You call that intimidating?!" Heinkel barked out a harsh laugh, wiping a tear from his eye. "Fuckin' hell, kid, you sound like you're running a daycare, not a prison camp! I've had hangovers worse than your hostage conditions!"

Kenichi let out a booming chuckle, grinning over at the embarrassed beastman. "I gotta say, Garfiel-kun, if that's your idea of being a bad guy, I'd hate to see you try to be nice. You'd probably end up giving them foot massages and reading them bedtime stories!"

"Garfiel-san is very considerate," Schult nodded sagely, completely missing the sarcasm as he looked at the blonde teenager with genuine respect. "Priscilla-sama only lets me take one nap a day."

"Schult, do not compare my divine generosity to the pathetic coddling of this mutt," Priscilla scoffed behind her fan, though her eyes danced with amusement at Garfiel's utter humiliation.

Even Ram, sitting in the back, let out a soft, fond sigh. "Truly, Garf is hopelessly pathetic when he tries to act cruel. A child playing dress-up as a demon."

"Stop it! All'a you!" Garfiel groaned, covering his face with both hands. Beside him, Otto was trying---and failing---to muffle his own laughter behind his hand.

Emilia and Subaru gain tense faces as he changes his expression to a hardened glare of resolve.

"This is our demand. Break the barrier surrounding this Sanctuary. The only one allowed to undergo the trial to do that is you. If you can't break it, we won't let any of the villagers leave. Of course, you can't leave here yourself, either!"

The laughter in the room quickly died down as the true weight of Garfiel's ultimatum settled over them once more. The terms of the hostages might have been comedic, but the reality of the burden placed on Emilia was crushing.

"So that's what this was all about," Wilhelm observed grimly, his seasoned eyes shifting to Roswaal. "The villagers weren't taken to be kept safe. They were taken to create a scenario where Emilia-sama would have no choice but to take the trial."

"And since Roswaal-sama conveniently injured himself attempting it..." Frederica murmured, her demi-human eyes narrowing in understanding. "It leaves Emilia-sama as the sole savior of the Sanctuary. It forces her to act as the hero to the very people who distrust her."

"He orchestrated the entire thing," Julius concluded, his voice laced with cold disgust. "A trial to break a Witch's barrier... To force a candidate for the throne into a corner just to further his own political agenda. Despicable."

Emilia stared at the screen, her earlier embarrassment forgotten as she watched her on-screen self process the immense pressure suddenly dropped onto her shoulders. She felt the warmth of Subaru's hand next to her, and the comforting grip of his mother on her other side.

The scene moves to show Subaru's expression moving to one of worry as it shows a chapel that has armed guards standing outside its doors. With shots playing of the inside, showing the rounded up villagers and their children looking at even more armed demihumans standing over them with anxious gazes.

The door creaks as it opens, letting the sunset light up the darkened church.

Subaru entered as villagers looked to him with surprise and even relief.

"Subaru-sama!" "You're safe!"

Naoko let out a small, breathless gasp, her hands flying to cover her mouth.

"Subaru-sama...?" Kenichi whispered, his eyes widening as he watched the scene unfold.

He let a confident smile show on his face as the villagers all stood up and huddled in front of him. "I'm glad you're all okay." He said to them.

"Are the others all right?" One of the villagers asked.

"Is the lord recovering from his injuries?" Another asks.

"We heard he got hurt trying to help us."

"We're truly relieved!"

"The lord got hurt trying to help them?" Heinkel scoffed loudly, an ugly sneer crossing his face. "Yeah, right. Help them right into a cage."

"Ignorance is a fragile bliss," Crusch noted softly, her eyes entirely devoid of warmth as she looked at Roswaal in the back row. "To think they worry so deeply for the man who orchestrated their confinement."

"It makes a guy sick," Garfiel spat, though whether he was angry at Roswaal, the situation, or his own past actions, he didn't elaborate. He just glared holes into the floorboards.

Naoko felt the tears breaching her eyelashes once more, but this time, they weren't born of horror or despair. "Look at them, Kenichi," she whispered, her voice trembling with an overwhelming swell of pride. "Look at how they look at him..."

"I see it," Kenichi replied, his voice thick with emotion. He stared at his son on the screen---the boy who had once shut himself in his room, paralyzed by the expectations of the world, now standing tall before dozens of people who looked at him as if he were their savior. "He's really... he's a man now, Naoko."

Subaru's smile was warm as he looked around at the crowd. "Okay, everyone, listen up."

"As you know, we got rid of the bad guy who attacked the village." Subaru's call for attention was met with awed gasps and wide smiles. "The other group that evacuated is back at the village, waiting for all of you to come home."

People's faces that were once warm with joy suddenly fell into hesitance and disappointment.

"I guess you've all been informed that it's not easy to get out of here." Subaru gave them all a look of comfort and encouragement. "We've got someone who's going to undergo the trial to set you all free!"

"You mean..." One of the villagers tried to speak to this, but another asked in his place.

"Are you going to do it?" She asked Subaru softly.

The question hung in the air, both on the screen and within the theater.

Emilia flinched.

It was a small, almost imperceptible jerk of her shoulders, but to those sitting close to her, it was as loud as a thunderclap. She lowered her head, her silver hair falling like a curtain to hide her face from the rest of the room. Her grip on Subaru's sleeping arm tightened just a fraction.

Rem, sitting directly behind her, noticed immediately. The maid's blue eyes softened with a mixture of sympathy and a harsh, undeniable truth.

They don't expect it to be Emilia-sama, Rem thought, her chest aching. They look at Subaru-kun, and they see the only one who can save them.

"Emilia-sama..." Petra whispered from a few rows back, having caught the half-elf's reaction.

"Why wouldn't they assume it's him?" Anastasia muttered, her sharp merchant's intuition picking apart the scene effortlessly. "He's the one who fought the cult. He's the one who came to check on 'em. In their eyes, he's the hero of their story."

"But it wasn't his duty," Wilhelm interjected quietly, his wise eyes fixed on the trembling form of the silver-haired girl in the front row. "It was the duty of their lord, and the duty of the royal candidate they sponsor. Yet, the burden falls to the boy."

"No, if I could do it, I'd be more than happy to, but..." Subaru moved to the side, bringing a hand to his hip in a reveal as the villagers all looked away from him to the doors.

"He really is trying his hardest to be a good wingman for her," Kenichi whispered with a warm, approving smile, watching his son set the stage. "Setting up the grand entrance so she doesn't have to do it alone."

Emilia walked inside with a brave expression, ignoring all the winces and slight murmurs that filled the chapel hall.

The warm smile on Kenichi's face faltered. Beside him, Naoko's posture stiffened as the collective flinch of the villagers echoed through the theater's high-quality speakers.

"I think you all know who this is." Subaru's voice rang out. "It's Emilia, the royal selection candidate. She'll be undergoing the trial."

The murmurs increased in velocity, and people started looking at each other openly now.

In the theater, the half-elf in question shrank slightly in her seat. Emilia's hands tightened around Subaru's sleeping arm, her amethyst eyes dropping to her lap. Even though she knew they had eventually accepted her, seeing that raw, unfiltered fear and hesitation directed at her past self still stung like a fresh wound.

"Why are they looking at her like that?" Naoko asked, her voice hushed but laced with genuine distress. She looked over at Emilia, seeing the girl's downcast expression. "She came here to save them, didn't she? Why are they wincing?"

"Because she's a half-elf with silver hair, Mother-san," Al answered from the row behind them, his voice lacking its usual playful tilt. "In this world, that's the equivalent of walking into a room wearing the face of the devil."

Naoko gasped softly, her eyes widening as she looked from the screen back to Emilia. Kenichi's jaw set tightly. They had heard passing mentions of the Witch's appearance before, but witnessing the casual, everyday prejudice their son's crush had to endure was entirely different.

"They are terrified of her," Rem bit out, her hands curling into tight fists on her lap. "Even after everything she and Subaru-kun have done for Arlam village, the deeply rooted superstitions of the world are not so easily washed away."

"I-It's okay, Rem," Emilia tried to placate her, though her voice wavered. "It's perfectly normal for them to be scared. I... I understand why."

"That doesn't make it right," Garfiel grunted from his seat, crossing his arms and glaring at the villagers on screen. As someone who had once judged her harshly in this very Sanctuary, the blonde brawler looked highly uncomfortable. "She's standin' right there offerin' her life on the line for 'em."

"I-I'm sorry you had to wait so long. In the place of your domain lord, Roswaal L. Mathers, I will undergo the trial." Emilia proclaimed herself before the villagers with uncertain resolve, clear on her face. She suddenly bowed her head deeply, her silver hair flying behind her. "I would appreciate your support!"

Emilia's hands were shown, shaking on her legs as she kept her head bowed.

"Hah!" Priscilla's mocking bark cut through the heavy air of the theater. She snapped her fan open, hiding her lips but doing nothing to conceal the arrogant amusement in her crimson eyes. "A candidate for the Dragon Throne bowing her head to filthy, ungrateful commoners? How utterly pathetic. A ruler does not beg for the support of those beneath her; she commands it. This is why you remain a half-wit playing dress-up, half-devil."

"Hold your tongue, Priscilla," Crusch's voice was like cracking ice. She didn't turn around, her amber eyes fixed on the bowing silver-haired girl on screen. "A true ruler takes responsibility for the plight of her people. The Margrave abandoned them to this prison. She is lowering her head not out of weakness, but out of a desperate, earnest desire to bear a burden she did not create. There is a distinct honor in that."

Wilhelm gave a slow, respectful nod of agreement from his seat. "A knightly display, Emilia-sama."

Priscilla merely scoffed, waving her fan dismissively, though she didn't push the issue further.

Emilia's face flushed red, and she brought her free hand up to hide her eyes. "Ah... um... p-please don't look at that part," she stammered, feeling entirely humiliated. To have her bravado stripped away, to have the entire room see just how terrified she truly was in that moment... it was mortifying.

"Emilia-sama..." Petra murmured, her young eyes welling up with tears as she saw the sheer terror the girl had been trying to suppress.

"You were terrified," Naoko said softly.

Emilia squeezed her eyes shut. "I was. I'm sorry. I know I'm supposed to be strong for them, but I was so scared. I didn't know what the trial was, and I knew they didn't trust me, and... and my hands just wouldn't stop shaking."

Suddenly, Emilia felt a warm, calloused hand gently pry her fingers away from her face. She opened her eyes to see Naoko leaning over the armrest, holding her hand with a maternal gentleness that made Emilia's breath hitch.

"Why are you apologizing, dear?" Naoko asked, giving Emilia's hand an affectionate squeeze.

"Because... because I look so weak," Emilia whispered, looking at her shaking hands on the giant screen.

"Weak?" Kenichi leaned forward to look past his wife, locking eyes with the half-elf. His goofy demeanor was completely gone, replaced by the steadfast, comforting presence of a father. "Emilia-chan, do you know what true courage is?"

Emilia blinked, slightly taken aback by the sudden shift in his tone. "N-No... what is it?"

"It's not about being fearless," Kenichi said, pointing a thumb at the screen. "A person who isn't scared to jump into danger isn't brave; they're just reckless. True courage is being absolutely terrified. It's having your hands shake, your heart pounding out of your chest, and every instinct screaming at you to run away..." He smiled warmly at her. "...And then doing it anyway."

Emilia's amethyst eyes widened, shining with unshed tears as Kenichi's words washed over her.

"You knew they were afraid of you, and you were terrified of what was coming," Naoko added, her voice thick with emotion. "But you still stood up there, and you still bowed, and you still offered to suffer for them. That's not weak at all. That makes you an incredibly strong girl."

"I..." Emilia choked on a sob, entirely unequipped to handle such pure, unfiltered parental validation. She looked down at Subaru, who slept peacefully in his seat, and then back up at his parents. Now she understood exactly where he got his bottomless well of kindness from.

"Thank you," Emilia whispered, wiping a stray tear from her cheek. "Thank you very much."

"Don't thank us for pointing out the obvious," Kenichi grinned, leaning back into his seat with his hands behind his head. "Though, I gotta say, Subaru definitely hit the jackpot with you. You're totally out of his league."

"Honey!" Naoko swatted his shoulder playfully, though she was smiling.

"What? I'm just being honest! He's gotta put in the work if he wants to keep up with a girl who's got that much guts!"

The heavy, melancholic atmosphere that had settled over the room lifted considerably. Even Rem found a small, genuine smile gracing her lips as she watched the exchange.

"Let me ask you just one thing." One voice rang out from between the villagers as an elder lady walked past people who were parting for her to make way to Emilia.

"Chief..." One of them whispered.

The older lady stood with her arms behind her, hunched back, looking at Emilia. "Why do this? You bowed your head to us the last time you saw us, too."

Images played of Emilia trying to talk to villagers with a pleading expression, but many were glancing at her with disgust and even fear. Women peaking behind their shoulders at her with weary expressions, and older men who were ignoring her openly, a man even held his broom over his head as a threat for Emilia to go away, while his wife held their child behind their house door.

"Goodness..." Naoko gasped softly, her hands flying to cover her mouth. "They're looking at her like she's a monster."

"To them, she might as well be, Naoko-san." Crusch spoke from the side, her tone lacking malice but heavy with the harsh reality of their world. "The prejudice against half-elves, particularly those with silver hair, is a deeply ingrained rot in our society."

Emilia shrank into her seat, her silver hair falling forward as she subconsciously tried to hide her face from the Natsuki parents. She had always known how the world saw her, but having Subaru's parents---people from a world without such hateful prejudices---watch her be treated like vermin brought a new, suffocating wave of shame over her.

"That's just cruel," Kenichi frowned, his jovial demeanor completely vanishing. He looked at the man raising the broom with genuine anger. "She's just a girl trying to help them. How can adults act like that to someone half their age?"

"Because fear makes fools of grown men, Old Guy," Felt scoffed, "I grew up in the slums. I've seen that look a million times. It's the look people give when they need a scapegoat for their miserable lives."

Even a last shot of Petra looking out of her window at Emilia, who stood all alone in the village.

In the row behind the knights, a choked, wet gasp echoed.

Petra had folded completely into herself. Her small hands gripped her maid uniform so tightly her knuckles were stark white. Tears were already spilling over her eyelashes, dropping onto her lap in heavy, miserable splatters.

"I... I'm sorry..." Petra sobbed, her voice cracking as the guilt she had been carrying finally broke through her enforced silence. "I was so scared of the stories... I shouldn't have closed the window on you, Emilia-sama... I'm so, so sorry..."

Frederica immediately wrapped an arm around the young girl, pulling her close and letting Petra bury her face in her chest.

Emilia's eyes widened, and she quickly turned around in her seat to face the weeping girl. "Petra, no, please don't cry!" Emilia pleaded, her own shame forgotten in the face of the girl's distress. "You didn't do anything wrong! I... I know I look scary. It's okay. You're here now, and you're helping us so much. I don't hold it against you at all, I promise!"

"She's far too forgiving," Ram muttered, though her eyes held a soft glint as she watched the half-elf comfort her junior.

"But we were the ones who turned you away." The elder looked at Emilia with a cold expression. "If it's because you want us to back you in the royal selection, that's just fine, so long as you come out and say so."

The elder woman's closed, ashen eyes were forced to open so she could stare at Emilia clearly. As two leaders of their own factions. "But if you have some other reason, we would like to know... that you are not a witch who is beyond our comprehension."

"Ah, the classic political shakedown," Anastasia hummed, leaning her chin on her hand, her merchant instincts flaring to life. "The elder's a smart cookie. She's giving Emilia-san an out, but also testing her. If she lies here, they'll know. So, what's the play, I wonder?"

"A ruler must be resolute in such moments," Julius noted, his pen pausing over his notebook. "If she shows weakness now, she will lose whatever fragile trust she has begun to build."

With this chance from the elder, Emilia noticed that the crowd had gone silent and looked to her with curious gazes, open ones, even Subaru gave her an encouraging expression with a nod to push her forward.

Kenichi let out a soft breath, a proud smile pulling at the corner of his lips. "There's my boy. Right in her corner."

"Always," Rem whispered from behind them. It was said with a mixture of melancholy and profound respect. Rem knew better than anyone the power of Subaru's unwavering belief.

She turned from Subaru with a cool expression as she answered truthfully.

"I don't have the confidence to give you a respectable answer right now. I don't have any strong, convincing words that would satisfy all of you. But... Though it wasn't for very long, I spent a few days with your families, the ones who aren't here now. And it made me realize... that families should be together."

Anastasia clicked her tongue. "Oof. Startin' off by admittin' weakness? That's a bold strategy. A bit too honest for the political stage, if ya ask me."

"Listen to her, Anastasia-sama," Crusch interrupted softly, her amber eyes fixed on the screen. "Sometimes, stripped of all grandiosity, honesty is the sharpest blade one can wield."

Beatrice shifted on Subaru's lap, her butterfly pupils tracking Emilia's hand on the screen. "... Bubby," the great spirit muttered softly, her voice carrying a rare, vulnerable tone.

The Natsuki parents froze. The words struck them like a physical blow.

Families should be together.

Naoko reached out blindly, her hand finding Kenichi's. He squeezed it tightly, his jaw clenching as he looked from the screen down to the sleeping face of his son. They were separated. Wrenched apart without warning, without goodbyes. The raw, simple truth in Emilia's words resonated so deeply within the Natsuki parents that it left an ache in their chests.

She unconsciously touched the green crystal on her chest, remembering that she, too, was now separated from her relatives.

"I want to return all of you to your families. I didn't promise them that I'd do so...But I promised myself. That's my only reason. I wasn't really thinking about earning your support or anything. But if possible, I'd like to... um...be friends with you all. That's what I want."

Silence blanketed the theater. It wasn't a tense silence, but a warm, heavy one.

"Emilia-sama..." The elder addressed her.

"Y-Yes?" Emilia held her hands together, straightening her back infront of the elder.

"I realize this will sound like we only care about what benefits us, but... I pray you will accept our friendship." With these words, the elder, followed by the rest of the residents, bowed their heads in front of Emilia.

"P-Please, I should ask the same! I know I'm not worthy, but please accept my friendship!" Emilia rattled excitedly and bowed again.

"I'm not worthy"? Who says that in this day and age?" Subaru gave a relieved smile from behind her, hands on his hips in an all's well that ends well vibe to celebrate the happy ending of Emilia's conversation with the villagers.

Emilia, sitting in her theater seat, let out a breath she hadn't realized she was holding. A soft, watery smile bloomed on her face as she watched the screen. To finally be accepted, not out of obligation or fear, but out of genuine warmth... it was everything she had ever fought for.

"Well I'll be," Felt grinned, crossing her arms. "She actually pulled it off. Didn't even need a fancy speech."

"It lacked the eloquence of a seasoned diplomat," Wilhelm observed, a rare, grandfatherly warmth in his eyes, "but it possessed the heart of a true protector. Well done, Emilia-sama."

"Hmph. A display of naive sentimentality that only worked because of the desperation of commoners," Priscilla fanned herself dismissively, though her red eyes lingered on Emilia for a moment longer than usual. "Still, one cannot argue with the results of the subjugation. They are yours now, half-devil."

"It wasn't subjugation," Emilia protested weakly, though the sting of Priscilla's words failed to penetrate her current relief.

Suddenly, a hand gently patted Emilia's shoulder.

She turned to see Naoko looking at her, tears shimmering in the older woman's eyes. Naoko didn't say a word, she simply offered a bright, watery smile that radiated absolute approval.

Beside her, Kenichi leaned over, giving Emilia a firm nod and a thumbs-up.

"You know, Emilia-chan," Kenichi said, his voice a bit thicker than usual. "Our boy can be a real idiot sometimes. Headstrong, reckless, and way too loud." He looked down at Subaru, ruffling his black hair affectionately before looking back up at the silver-haired girl.
